```
# Todo

This file contains near term tasks with a short description
and reference links to more details.

## In Progress



## Todo

A markdown list of tasks to do in the near future, ordered
highest-priority first. Keep entries brief — 1-3 lines.
Detailed motivation, safety requirements, and ordering belong
in `notes/chores-NN.md` design subsections; link via `[N]` ref.

Items use lazy numbering — every entry begins with `1. `; the
markdown renderer auto-numbers them, so reorder/insert without
renumbering. Reference by displayed number ("let's work on #3").

1. vc-x1 commands: support single and dual repo. Generalize via
   `--scope=app|other|both` across all commands; foundational
   for new commands and for retrofitting sync / push / finalize
   under one vocabulary. [60]
1. vc-x1 push: `--scope=app|claude|both` flag. Applies the
   generalized convention; warn on scope/WC mismatch. [57],[60]
1. vc-x1 push: `--squash` flag. Squashes WC into `@-` via
   `--ignore-immutable` and force-pushes; needs
   `--force-with-lease`-equivalent + state-sanity preflight in
   place first. [57]
1. vc-x1 push: `--message-file PATH` flag. Git-style commit
   message file (first line = title, blank, rest = body).
   Alternative to `--title` + `--body`. [58]
1. Mirror `--check` / `--no-check` onto `vc-x1 push` (forwards
   through to the preflight `vc-x1 sync` invocation).
   0.37.1 hard-codes `--check`; default stays `--check`.
1. Add `status` (alias `st`) subcommand: `jj st` across both
   repos in one shot. Uses `--scope` from day one. Natural
   home for the working-copy signal called out in [54].
1. Add `validate-repo` subcommand: diagnostic that runs all
   `verify_*` checks (tracking, push state freshness, ochid
   integrity, conflicts, config sanity, working-copy state)
   and reports per-check pass/fail. Exit code = number of
   failed checks. Implementation: promote
   `verify_state_sanity` / `verify_completion_sanity` from
   push.rs to `common.rs`. [69]
1. sync: surface working-copy state in the up-to-date summary
   (per-repo pending-files count or compact stat). Wording-only
   fix shipped in 0.37.1; this is the design+impl. [54]
1. bm-track silent-when-clean refinement. Print on entry/exit
   only when state isn't fully tracked or when exit state
   differs from entry. [62]
1. "Oh shit" revert — post-success undo via `.vc-x1-ops/`
   anchor dir. Idea-stage; every repo-mutating command drops a
   pre-op snapshot, `vc-x1 undo` restores both repos. [57]
1. vc-x1 test-fixture should refuse `--path` values that resolve
   inside the current workspace root (error or warn). Dogfood
   surfaced this: `--path ./tf-1` inside the repo let jj
   snapshot the fixture's bare-git remotes — 56-file noise blob.
1. Restructure templates: replace separate `vc-template-x1` +
```

> AGENT

Acquainted. Here's the lay of the land:

**Project**: `vc-x1` — a Rust CLI (v0.39.0, edition 2024) wrapping `jj` for dual-repo workflows (app repo + `.claude` session repo). Last release "push hardening cycle" just landed on main.

**Source** (`src/`): subcommands `chid`, `clone`, `desc`, `finalize`, `fix_desc`, `init`, `list`, `push`, `show`, `symlink`, `sync`, `validate_desc`, `test_fixture` + helpers (`common`, `desc_helpers`, `logging`, `toml_simple`, `test_helpers`).

**Working copy**:
- App repo: clean, `@` empty on `main` at `f6369792`.
- `.claude` repo: active session writes (expected — per CLAUDE.md, `.claude` always has uncommitted changes during a session).

**Todo queue** (top of `notes/todo.md`): `--scope=app|other|both` generalization is the big foundational item; several `push` flags queued behind it (`--squash`, `--message-file`, mirrored `--check`/`--no-check`); plus `status`/`st` and `validate-repo` subcommands, bm-track refinement, and an "oh shit" revert idea.

**Key guardrails from CLAUDE.md** I'm holding:
- `-R .` / `-R .claude` for every jj command; don't `cd`.
- `vc-x1 push` wraps commit→bookmark→push→finalize with two approval gates (review, message).
- After `finalize`: hard stop the turn — no prose, no tools, wait for user.
- Pre-commit checklist: fmt, clippy, test, install, retest, update `todo.md` / `chores-*.md` / `README.md`.
- Version bump first; `X.Y.Z-N` for multi-step, `X.Y.Z` for single-step.
- Doc comments on every `.rs` file/fn/method; `// OK: …` on every non-test `unwrap*`.
- "The bot thinks …" prefix for speculation in durable text.

Ready for direction.

```
51	## Generalize --scope to all commands (design)
52	
53	Surfaced 2026-04-23 (from the user, top of todo): vc-x1 commands
54	should support both single and dual repo modes. The technique
55	sketched for `vc-x1 push` (`--scope=app|claude|both`) is the
56	right shape, but the scope concept should be a project-wide
57	convention rather than push-only.
58	
59	**Naming:** `--scope=app|other|both` — "other" rather than
60	"claude" because the second repo isn't always `.claude`; could
61	be a different bot or a different sibling project structure.
62	
63	**Why scope-first** (before adding more commands or push
64	features): each new command that operates across both repos
65	would otherwise need its own ad-hoc handling. Locking in the
66	convention now means new commands inherit it for free, and
67	existing commands (sync, finalize, push) get retrofitted under
68	the same vocabulary.
69	
70	**Rough scope of the change:**
71	
72	- Shared CLI flag definition + parsing helper (likely in
73	  `common.rs`).
74	- `sync`: already accepts `-R` for single-repo. Migrate to
75	  `--scope`; alias or deprecate `-R` (TBD below).
76	- `push`: `--scope` per the existing design, with
77	  warn-on-WC-mismatch.
78	- `finalize`: already takes `--repo`; rationalize naming.
79	- New commands (`status` etc.) start with `--scope` from day 1.
80	
81	**Open:** do we deprecate `-R` immediately, alias it, or keep
82	both indefinitely? Probably: alias for one minor cycle,
83	deprecate, remove. Decide before implementation.
84	
85	## Push hardening: state + stage sanity (design)
86	
87	Two related correctness fixes for `vc-x1 push` resume behavior.
88	Both surfaced 2026-04-22 from a 0.37.1 dogfood incident and are
89	described together because they tackle the same failure mode
90	(silent false success after stale state).
91	
92	**The incident:** 0.37.1 push errored at finalize-claude;
93	out-of-band recovery (manual finalize + force-push of a squash)
94	moved the world forward; 0.37.2 push then resumed at the parked
95	finalize-claude, no-op'd, and falsely declared "completed all
96	stages" while working copies still held uncommitted changes.
97	
98	**State-sanity preflight on resume.** Before any stage runs,
99	verify saved state matches reality:
100	
101	- `state.app_chid` still exists (not abandoned/rewritten).
102	- `main` bookmark at `state.app_chid`'s commit.
103	- `state.claude_chid` consistent with `.claude` working copy.
104	
105	On mismatch, refuse with a loud "state is stale — run
106	`vc-x1 push --restart`" error.
107	
108	**Stage-prereq verification + honest completion.** Each stage
109	declares what it expects (working-copy dirty for commit-app;
110	bookmark at specific commit for bookmark-both; etc.); the
111	dispatcher checks before running. "Completed all stages" should
112	only print when stages genuinely ran or were
113	verified-already-done, not when they were skipped without
114	verification.
115	
116	**Implementation order:** state-sanity first (broader guard),
117	then per-stage prereqs (more invasive). Both should land before
118	the `--squash` work to avoid building on a known-fragile resume
119	path.
120	
121	## bm-track silent-when-clean (design)
122	
123	Today bm-track prints two lines per command (entry + exit)
124	unconditionally. In steady state both lines say
125	`tracked,tracked` — pure noise. Defer-flagged in 0.37.4 until
126	signal-confidence was established through more dogfood; several
127	0.37.x cycles have now used the probe with no false positives
128	or missed regressions.
129	
130	**Proposed behavior:**
131	
132	- Always probe on entry and exit (no change).
133	- Print on entry only when state isn't fully tracked.
134	- Print on exit when state isn't fully tracked OR when the exit
135	  state differs from entry.
136	- When printing on exit, include the entry state in the output
137	  so the transition is explicit:
138	  `bm-track vc-x1 <cmd>: enter=<state> → exit=<state>`.
139	
140	The entry-state-in-exit-output requirement is important —
141	"entry was ok (not printed), exit failed" is inference;
142	"entry=tracked, exit=NOT_TRACKED" in one line is evidence.
143	
144	**Implementation note:** record entry state in a local variable
145	at the enter call site and pass into the exit call.
146	
147	## Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection (design)
148	
149	Diagnosed 2026-04-22 dogfood: jj's tracking state is
150	**per-workspace** (local `.jj` store), not shared via git refs.
151	Sync across machines transfers refs but not the tracking flag —
152	so a fresh workspace fetched-into never auto-tracks. The failure
153	surfaces only when `jj git push` is attempted, which is too late
154	(push-app already succeeded in our case).
155	
156	**Policy (decided):** error loudly, with the exact
157	`jj bookmark track <b> --remote=<r> -R <repo>` remediation
158	command. No self-heal — keeps the fix explicit and visible.
159	
160	**Scope (every command that creates or mutates repo state):**
161	
162	- `vc-x1 init`: already correct (Step 10 calls `jj bookmark
163	  track`). Add the check as a post-condition sanity assertion
164	  anyway.
165	- `vc-x1 clone`: does `git clone` then `jj git init --colocate`.
166	  Whether that combination auto-establishes tracking in jj's
167	  workspace store is unclear; probably needs an explicit
168	  `jj bookmark track` for each cloned bookmark after the init.
169	  Verify and fix.
170	- `vc-x1 test-fixture`: Step 7's `jj git push --bookmark main`
171	  establishes tracking as a side effect of the first push.
172	  Works correctly — confirmed via `jj bookmark list --tracked`
173	  on a fresh fixture. No change needed; the post-condition
174	  sanity check would naturally cover it.
175	- `vc-x1 sync` preflight: detect + error.
176	- `vc-x1 push` preflight: detect + error (before any mutation).
177	- `vc-x1 finalize`: detect + error before the squash, so a
178	  failed push doesn't leave a half-finalized state.
179	
180	**Shared helper:** `common::verify_tracking(repo, bookmark,
181	remote) -> Result<(), Err>` or similar. Probably use
182	`jj bookmark list --tracked -T <template>` under the hood
183	rather than parsing human-readable output.
184	
185	## Scope design refinements (0.37.8)
```

```
185	## Scope design refinements (0.37.8)
186	
187	Notes-only release. Captures six refinements to the scope and
188	squash designs ([57], [60]) from a 2026-04-23 dogfood discussion
189	(the manual squash workflow used to fold late changes into the
190	0.37.7 commit, plus follow-up Q&A on cwd independence and
191	plain-old-repo handling).
192	
193	**Scope semantic invariant.** With `--scope=app|other|both`
194	(per [60]):
195	
196	- `--scope=both` (or omitted; `both` is the default) → dual-repo
197	  operation.
198	- `--scope=app` → single-repo operation on the app repo (direct
199	  reference).
200	- `--scope=other` → single-repo operation on the "other" repo
201	  (indirect reference; resolves via `.vc-config.toml`'s
202	  `[workspace].other-repo` field, which already exists).
203	
204	Anything that isn't `both` is single-repo. The dispatcher branches
205	once on this invariant; new commands inheriting `--scope` get the
206	semantic for free.
207	
208	**`--other` is project config, not a per-command flag.** Came up
209	during the same discussion: should there be a `--other=PATH` flag
210	to override the project's "other" repo per command? Answer: no.
211	That belongs in `.vc-config.toml` (already there as
212	`[workspace].other-repo`). Keeping `--other` out of the
213	per-command surface keeps the flag set small and the per-command
214	vocabulary uniform across all commands — "other" always resolves
215	the same way.
216	
217	**Cwd independence + counterpart-rename suggestion.** Scope names
218	are workspace-anchored, not cwd-relative. Each repo has its own
219	`.vc-config.toml` self-describing its role:
220	
221	- App's config: `[workspace] path = "/" other-repo = ".claude"`.
222	- `.claude`'s config: `[workspace] path = "/.claude"
223	  other-repo = ".."`.
224	
225	`path = "/"` identifies the app repo (only the app has it). The
226	dispatcher reads the local config, determines "am I app or other?"
227	via `path`, then resolves `--scope=app` and `--scope=other` to the
228	right physical paths regardless of cwd. From `cd .claude`:
229	
230	- `--scope=app` → `..` (the app repo, found via `.claude`'s
231	  `other-repo`).
232	- `--scope=other` → `.` (`.claude` itself, since `path != "/"`
233	  → I AM the workspace's "other").
234	
235	Naming gotcha: the config field `other-repo` means "this repo's
236	counterpart" (cwd-flavored direction; from `.claude` it's `..` =
237	the app), while CLI `--scope=other` means "the workspace's 'other'
238	role" (workspace-anchored; always = `.claude`). They coincide
239	from app's cwd (both physically = `.claude`) but diverge from
240	`.claude`'s cwd (config `other-repo` = `..`, CLI `--scope=other`
241	= `.`). Consider renaming the config field to `counterpart` or
242	`peer` to disambiguate from the CLI scope name.
243	
244	**Plain-old-repo handling: three workspace states.** vc-x1 should
245	be practical without a full workspace too. Three distinguishable
246	states, all described by `.vc-config.toml` presence/contents:
247	
248	- **POR** — no `.vc-config.toml`. Pure git/jj repo, vc-x1
249	  doesn't know it exists. Implicit `--scope=app`;
250	  `--scope=other/both` errors with "not in a vc-x1 workspace
251	  (no `.vc-config.toml`) — drop --scope or use --scope=app".
252	- **Single-repo workspace** — `.vc-config.toml` with `path`
253	  only (no `other-repo`). vc-x1-aware, no companion. Example:
254	  `vc-template-x1`. Implicit `--scope=app`; `--scope=other/both`
255	  errors with "no other-repo configured. Add `other-repo = …`
256	  to `.vc-config.toml` to enable dual-repo operations".
257	- **Dual-repo workspace** — `.vc-config.toml` with `path` +
258	  `other-repo`. Full setup. Example: `vc-x1` itself.
259	  `--scope=app/other/both` all valid.
260	
261	Edge cases: a `.vc-config.toml` with `path != "/"` and no
262	`other-repo` (e.g. `path = "/.claude"`) — the repo identifies as
263	the "other" side but its companion is missing. Error: "config
264	identifies this as `/.claude` but no `other-repo` to point at the
265	app side; companion is missing." Dual-repo with `other-repo`
266	pointing somewhere that doesn't exist → loud error (workspace
267	state corrupted), not POR fallback.
268	
269	**`vc-x1 push --squash` composition.** With `--scope` in place,
270	the squash design (per [57]) composes naturally:
271	
272	- `vc-x1 push --squash` (no scope) → squash both repos and
273	  force-push both. End-to-end version of the manual two-step
274	  recipe in CLAUDE.md "Late changes after push".
275	- `vc-x1 push --squash --scope=app` → squash app only.
276	- `vc-x1 push --squash --scope=other` → squash other only.
277	
278	**Dogfood validation (2026-04-23).** The manual recipe was applied
279	successfully to both repos in sequence to fold late changes into
280	the 0.37.7 commit. Observations worth carrying into the `--squash`
281	implementation:
282	
283	- jj's `jj squash --ignore-immutable` preserves the changeID of
284	  the squashed-into commit. Both repos' ochid trailers stayed
285	  valid through the squash without any fixup. The future
286	  `--squash` implementation can rely on this — no ochid rewrite
287	  needed.
288	- The trimmed "Late changes after push" recipe (0.37.6, two
289	  lines for the `@-` case) worked identically on both sides;
290	  no `jj bookmark set` needed since the bookmark moves with the
291	  rewritten commit.
292	- Push state was already cleared at the end of the prior
293	  successful push (state reported "completed all stages (state
294	  cleared)"). The post-recovery `--restart` / `rm` step
295	  documented in CLAUDE.md "Manual finalize fallback" wasn't
296	  needed in this case — but the doc remains correct for cases
297	  where prior push didn't clean up.
298	
299	- `notes/chores-06.md` — this subsection.
300	- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry + new `[65]` reference + new
301	  Todo entry for the template restructure (vc-template-x1 +
302	  `.claude/` subdir, captured separately for later
303	  implementation).
304	
305	## Bookmark tracking verification (0.38.0)
306	
307	Cross-command tracking verification per the [63] design — every
308	repo-modifying command checks its target bookmark for non-tracking
309	remote refs and errors with the exact remediation command if any
```

```
28	/// Banner string emitted as the first line of normal command runs
29	/// and shown at the top of subcommand `--help` output. Built from
30	/// `Cargo.toml`'s name + version at compile time so it stays in
31	/// sync with the bumped version.
32	const BANNER: &str = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
33	
34	/// Top-level about line — name, version, and the project tagline
35	/// on a single line. Used as the top-level `about` so `vc-x1 -h`
36	/// reads as one banner-plus-tagline header instead of two stacked
37	/// lines.
38	const TOP_ABOUT: &str = concat!(
39	    env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"),
40	    " ",
41	    env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
42	    " - jj workspace tooling"
43	);
44	
45	/// Build the clap command tree with `BANNER` set as `before_help`
46	/// on every subcommand (transitively). Top-level skips `before_help`
47	/// because its own `about` already carries the name+version+tagline.
48	/// Walks via `mut_subcommand` so individual subcommand
49	/// `#[command(long_about = ...)]` blocks don't have to repeat the
50	/// banner text.
51	fn cli_with_banner() -> clap::Command {
52	    fn add_to_subs(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
53	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
54	            .get_subcommands()
55	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
56	            .collect();
57	        for name in names {
58	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
59	        }
60	        cmd
61	    }
62	    fn add_with_banner(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
63	        cmd = cmd.before_help(BANNER);
64	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
65	            .get_subcommands()
66	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
67	            .collect();
68	        for name in names {
69	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
70	        }
71	        cmd
72	    }
73	    add_to_subs(Cli::command())
74	}
75	
76	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
77	#[command(
78	    version,
79	    propagate_version = true,
80	    about = TOP_ABOUT,
81	    max_term_width = 80
82	)]

97	pub(crate) enum Commands {
98	    /// Print the changeID for a revision
99	    Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
100	
101	    /// Show full description of a commit
102	    Desc(desc::DescArgs),
103	
104	    /// List commits in a jj repo
105	    List(list::ListArgs),
106	
107	    /// Show commit details and diff summary
108	    Show(show::ShowArgs),

110	    /// Validate commit descriptions against the other repo
111	    #[command(

113	        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
114	        Status labels:\n  \

116	          err  — ochid has issues (wrong prefix, wrong length, ID not found)\n  \
117	          miss — no ochid trailer; shows match from other repo if found"
118	    )]
119	    ValidateDesc(validate_desc::ValidateDescArgs),
120

122	    #[command(long_about = "Fix commit descriptions against the other repo.\n\n\
123	        Default is dry-run; use --no-dry-run to write changes.\n\n\
124	        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
125	        Status labels:\n  \
126	          ok    — ochid trailer is valid (no change)\n  \
127	          fix   — ochid has issues, shows proposed fix (dry-run)\n  \
128	          fixed — ochid was rewritten (--no-dry-run)\n  \
129	          add   — missing ochid, match found, shows proposed addition (dry-run)\n  \
130	          added — missing ochid was added (--no-dry-run)\n  \
131	          skip  — skipped (no ochid, no match, or max-fixes reached)\n  \
132	          err   — ID not found and no --fallback provided")]
133	    FixDesc(fix_desc::FixDescArgs),
134	
135	    /// Clone a dual-repo project
136	    Clone(clone::CloneArgs),
137	
138	    /// Create a new dual-repo project
139	    Init(init::InitArgs),
140	
141	    /// Create Claude Code project symlink
142	    Symlink(symlink::SymlinkArgs),
143	
144	    /// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes
145	    #[command(long_about = "Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n\n\
146	        Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and\n\
147	        `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo\n\
148	        projects or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces. Flag is repeatable\n\
149	        or comma-separated (e.g. `-R . -R .claude` or `-R .,.claude`).\n\n\
150	        Default is --check (verify only) — fatal if any repo needs\n\
151	        action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).\n\
152	        Per repo:\n  \
153	          - up-to-date        nothing to do\n  \
154	          - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote\n  \
155	          - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)\n  \
156	          - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts\n  \
157	          - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip\n\n\
158	        Scripts and automation should pass --check or --no-check\n\
159	        explicitly — defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the\n\
160	        contract. Interactive use can rely on the default.\n\n\
161	        After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly\n\
162	        advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing\n\
163	        working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up\n\
164	        orphaned on a stale branch.\n\n\
165	        On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via\n\
166	        `jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the\n\
167	        revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.\n\n\
168	        Output shape:\n  \
169	          - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)\n  \
170	          - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error\n  \
171	          - action needed (--no-check): per-repo fetch + state + actions\n  \
172	          - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success")]
173	    Sync(sync::SyncArgs),
174	
175	    /// Squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo
176	    #[command(long_about = "Squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo.\n\n\
177	        Designed for the bot to atomically finalize its session repo:\n\
178	        --detach exits immediately, --delay waits for trailing writes,\n\
179	        --squash folds them in, --bookmark + --push sends it upstream.\n\
180	        Every flag is opt-in. See README.md for details.")]
181	    Finalize(finalize::FinalizeArgs),
182	
183	    /// Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command
184	    #[command(
185	        long_about = "Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.\n\n\
186	        Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a\n\
187	        single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a\n\
188	        state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can\n\
189	        resume without re-doing completed stages.\n\n\
190	        Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)\n\
191	        → message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →\n\
192	        commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both\n\
193	        → push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /\n\
194	        commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via\n\
195	        `jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.\n\
196	        After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and\n\
197	        recovery is forward-only.\n\n\
198	        Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes\n\
199	        to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body\n\
200	        across resumes so only the first invocation needs them."
201	    )]
202	    Push(push::PushArgs),
203	
204	    /// Create a throwaway dual-repo jj fixture (local remotes; see README)
205	    TestFixture(test_fixture::TestFixtureArgs),
206	
207	    /// Remove a test fixture (safety: name must start with vc-x1-test-)
208	    TestFixtureRm(test_fixture::TestFixtureRmArgs),
209	}

211	fn run_command(result: Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>) -> ExitCode {
212	    match result {
213	        Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,
214	        Err(e) => {
215	            error!("{e}");
216	            ExitCode::FAILURE
217	        }
218	    }
219	}
220	
221	/// Permanent sanity check for the `main`-bookmark tracking state
222	/// in both repos of the dual-repo workspace. Prints one line on
223	/// entry and one on exit of every command. If entry and exit
224	/// differ, the executing command is the culprit; if entry differs
225	/// from the previous command's exit, something *between*
226	/// invocations broke it. Originally added in 0.37.2 as a temporary
227	/// diagnostic; promoted to permanent in 0.37.4 after the user
228	/// reported "happens more than once" — the signal is worth keeping
229	/// in place until confidence in its correctness is well-established
230	/// across varied use, at which point a "silent when clean"
231	/// refinement would remove the steady-state noise without losing
232	/// detection value (see `notes/todo.md`).
233	///
234	/// Walks up from cwd to locate the workspace root (the directory
235	/// whose `.vc-config.toml` has `path = "/"`), then probes `<root>`
236	/// and `<root>/.claude`. Same labeling whether the user runs from
237	/// the app root, from `.claude`, or from any subdir.
238	fn bm_track(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {
239	    let header = format!("bm-track {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}");
240	    let root = match find_workspace_root() {
241	        Some(r) => r,
242	        None => {
243	            log::info!("{header}: no-workspace");
244	            return;
245	        }
246	    };
247	    let repos: [(std::path::PathBuf, &str); 2] =
248	        [(root.clone(), "app"), (root.join(".claude"), ".claude")];
249	    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
250	    for (repo, label) in repos {
251	        if !repo.join(".jj").exists() {
252	            parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=no-jj"));
253	            continue;
254	        }
255	        match bm_track_one(&repo, "main", "origin") {
256	            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=tracked")),
257	            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED")),
258	            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(
259	                "{label}(main)=err({})",
260	                e.lines().next().unwrap_or("")
261	            )),
262	        }
263	    }
264	    log::info!("{header}: {}", parts.join(", "));
265	}
266	
267	/// Walk up from cwd looking for `.vc-config.toml` with `path = "/"`.
268	/// That's the workspace root — the app-repo side of the dual-repo
269	/// pair. Returns `None` if no such config is found up to filesystem
270	/// root (e.g., running from outside any vc-x1 workspace).
271	fn find_workspace_root() -> Option<std::path::PathBuf> {
272	    let mut cur = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
273	    loop {
274	        let config = cur.join(".vc-config.toml");
275	        if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&config) {
276	            // Match either `path = "/"` or `path="/"` (with/without spaces).
277	            let compact: String = content
278	                .lines()
279	                .find(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with("path"))
280	                .unwrap_or("") // OK: no `path =` line ⇒ empty match, continues walking up
281	                .chars()
282	                .filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace())
283	                .collect();
284	            if compact.starts_with("path=\"/\"") {
285	                return Some(cur);
286	            }
287	        }
288	        cur = cur.parent()?.to_path_buf();
289	    }
290	}
291	
292	/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.
293	/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` shows
294	/// an `@<remote>` line (in any form — `@origin:` when synced, or
295	/// `@origin (ahead by N commits):` / similar when divergent — both
296	/// still count as tracking). `Ok(false)` when no such line exists
297	/// (bookmark isn't tracking this remote or doesn't exist). `Err` on
298	/// subprocess failure.
299	fn bm_track_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {
300	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
301	    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
302	        .args(["bookmark", "list", "--tracked", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str])
303	        .output()
304	        .map_err(|e| format!("spawn: {e}"))?;
305	    if !output.status.success() {
306	        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());
307	    }
308	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
309	    // Accept both the synced form (`@origin:`) and the decorated
310	    // divergent form (`@origin (ahead by N commits):`), which still
311	    // represents a tracking relationship.
312	    let colon = format!("@{remote}:");
313	    let paren = format!("@{remote} ");
314	    Ok(stdout.lines().any(|l| {
315	        let t = l.trim_start();
316	        t.starts_with(&colon) || t.starts_with(&paren)
317	    }))
318	}
319	
320	fn main() -> ExitCode {
321	    CompleteEnv::with_factory(cli_with_banner).complete();
322	    let matches = cli_with_banner().get_matches();
323	    let cli = match Cli::from_arg_matches(&matches) {
324	        Ok(cli) => cli,
325	        Err(e) => {
326	            e.exit();
327	        }
328	    };
329	
330	    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
331	    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());
332	
333	    // The detached `finalize --exec` re-entry is the bot's
334	    // session-end child; it shouldn't print the banner (extra
335	    // chatter in its log) or surface user-facing failure markers
336	    // (those are meant for the user's next interactive run).
337	    let is_detached_exec = matches!(
338	        cli.command,
339	        Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
340	    );
341	
342	    // `-L` / `--no-label` on the read-only multi-repo subcommands
343	    // (chid, desc, list, show) makes their output script-parseable
344	    // — adding a leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` line would break that. Skip
345	    // the banner whenever the active subcommand has the flag set.
346	    let suppress_banner = match &cli.command {
347	        Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
348	        Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
349	        Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
350	        Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
351	        _ => false,
352	    };
353	
354	    if !is_detached_exec {
355	        if !suppress_banner {
356	            // Banner on every normal run, mirroring what `--help`
357	            // shows at the top.
358	            log::info!("{BANNER}");
359	        }
360	        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
361	    }
362	
363	    // Command name for bm-track output (first positional arg after
364	    // the binary; clap has already validated it by the time we get here).
365	    let command_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".to_string()); // OK: default when somehow invoked without a subcommand
366	
367	    if !is_detached_exec {
368	        bm_track("enter", &command_name);
369	    }
370	
371	    let exit_code = match cli.command {
372	        Commands::Chid(chid_args) => run_command(chid::chid(&chid_args)),
373	        Commands::Desc(desc_args) => run_command(desc::desc(&desc_args)),
374	        Commands::List(list_args) => run_command(list::list(&list_args)),
375	        Commands::Show(show_args) => run_command(show::show(&show_args)),
376	        Commands::ValidateDesc(validate_desc_args) => {
377	            run_command(validate_desc::validate_desc(&validate_desc_args))
378	        }
379	        Commands::FixDesc(fix_desc_args) => run_command(fix_desc::fix_desc(&fix_desc_args)),
380	        Commands::Clone(clone_args) => run_command(clone::clone_repo(&clone_args)),
381	        Commands::Init(init_args) => run_command(init::init(&init_args)),
382	        Commands::Symlink(symlink_args) => run_command(symlink::symlink(&symlink_args)),
383	        Commands::Sync(sync_args) => run_command(sync::sync(&sync_args)),
384	        Commands::Finalize(finalize_args) => {
385	            let opts = match finalize_args.into_opts(cli.log) {
386	                Ok(opts) => opts,
387	                Err(e) => {
388	                    error!("{e}");
389	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
390	                }
391	            };
392	            run_command(finalize::finalize(&opts))
393	        }
394	        Commands::TestFixture(args) => run_command(test_fixture::test_fixture(&args)),
395	        Commands::TestFixtureRm(args) => run_command(test_fixture::test_fixture_rm(&args)),
396	        Commands::Push(push_args) => run_command(push::push(&push_args)),
397	    };
398	
399	    if !is_detached_exec {
400	        bm_track("exit ", &command_name);
401	    }
402	
403	    exit_code
404	}
405

413	            .unwrap_err()
414	            .to_string();
415	        assert!(err.contains("bogus"));
416	    }
417	}
```

```
61	/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
62	///
63	/// Counts are `Some(0)` for closed sides and `None` for open (dotted) sides.
64	/// Open sides become unlimited unless a positional count is applied later.
65	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
66	pub struct DotSpec {
67	    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
68	    pub rev: String,
69	    /// Descendant count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
70	    pub desc_count: Option<usize>,
71	    /// Ancestor count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
72	    pub anc_count: Option<usize>,
73	}
74	
75	/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
76	///
77	/// Returns the bare revision with open/closed counts per side.
78	/// `None` means the side had dots (open for expansion);
79	/// `Some(0)` means no dots on that side (closed).
80	/// Actual counts are applied later from positional args.
81	pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
82	    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
83	        DotSpec {
84	            rev: inner.to_string(),
85	            desc_count: None,
86	            anc_count: None,
87	        }
88	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
89	        DotSpec {
90	            rev: inner.to_string(),
91	            desc_count: None,
92	            anc_count: Some(0),
93	        }
94	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
95	        DotSpec {
96	            rev: inner.to_string(),
97	            desc_count: Some(0),
98	            anc_count: None,
99	        }
100	    } else {
101	        DotSpec {
102	            rev: rev.to_string(),
103	            desc_count: Some(0),
104	            anc_count: Some(0),
105	        }
106	    }
107	}
108	
109	/// Resolve positional and flag args into a `DotSpec` ready for `collect_ids`.
110	///
111	/// Handles: dot notation parsing, flag vs positional precedence,
112	/// count-means-total semantics (subtracts 1 for anchor).
113	pub fn resolve_spec(
114	    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
115	    pos_count: Option<usize>,
116	    flag_rev: &str,
117	    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
118	    default_rev: &str,
119	) -> DotSpec {
120	    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
121	    let rev_str = if flag_rev_set {
122	        flag_rev
123	    } else {
124	        pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev) // OK: positional absent → fall back to CLI default
125	    };
126	    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
127	
128	    let count = if flag_limit.is_some() {
129	        flag_limit
130	    } else {
131	        pos_count
132	    };
133	
134	    if let Some(c) = count {
135	        let n = c.saturating_sub(1);
136	        let both_open = spec.desc_count.is_none() && spec.anc_count.is_none();
137	        if both_open {
138	            // Split budget across both sides: ancestors get the extra
139	            let desc_n = n / 2;
140	            let anc_n = n - desc_n;
141	            spec.desc_count = Some(desc_n);
142	            spec.anc_count = Some(anc_n);
143	        } else {
144	            if spec.desc_count.is_none() {
145	                spec.desc_count = Some(n);
146	            }
147	            if spec.anc_count.is_none() {
148	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
149	            }
150	            // bare `x 5` → treat as `x.. 5` (ancestors)
151	            if spec.desc_count == Some(0) && spec.anc_count == Some(0) {
152	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
153	            }
154	        }
155	    }
156	
157	    spec
158	}

160	/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.
161	///
162	/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:
163	/// - **stderr at `debug!`** on success — jj's chatter (`Moved 1 bookmarks
164	///   to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) is hidden by default
165	///   and surfaced with `-v`. The debug formatter indents each line two
166	///   spaces so subprocess output sits visually under the caller's own
167	///   `info!` line in `-v` mode.
168	/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data
169	///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), and `info!` would flood the user
170	///   with machine-readable output they didn't ask for.
171	/// - **Failures propagate as `Err`** carrying the stderr; the caller's
172	///   error handler (`main::run_command`) surfaces it at `error!`.
173	pub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
174	    let args_str = args.join(" ");
175	    debug!("$ {cmd} {args_str}");
176	    trace!("cwd: {}", cwd.display());
177	    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)
178	        .args(args)
179	        .current_dir(cwd)
180	        .output()
181	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run {cmd}: {e}"))?;
182	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
183	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
184	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
185	        debug!("{stdout}");
186	    }
187	    if !output.status.success() {
188	        return Err(format!("{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}").into());
189	    }
190	    if !stderr.is_empty() {
191	        debug!("{stderr}");
192	    }
193	    Ok(stdout)
194	}
195	
196	/// Create a directory (and parents). Logs at debug level.
197	pub fn mkdir_p(path: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
198	    debug!("mkdir -p {}", path.display());
199	    std::fs::create_dir_all(path)?;
200	    Ok(())
201	}
202	
203	/// Write content to a file. Logs at debug level.
204	pub fn write_file(path: &Path, content: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
205	    debug!("write {}", path.display());
206	    std::fs::write(path, content)?;
207	    Ok(())
208	}

210	/// Display a prompt, read a line from stdin, and log both together.
211	/// Returns the trimmed response.
212	pub fn prompt(msg: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
213	    use std::io::Write;
214	    eprint!("{msg}");
215	    std::io::stderr().flush()?;
216	    let mut response = String::new();
217	    std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut response)?;
218	    let trimmed = response.trim().to_string();
219	    info!("{msg}{trimmed}");
220	    Ok(trimmed)
221	}

223	/// Wrap text in ANSI bold escape codes.
224	pub fn bold(s: &str) -> String {
225	    format!("\x1b[1m{s}\x1b[0m")
226	}
227	
228	/// Bold only the first line of a multi-line string.
229	pub fn bold_first_line(s: &str) -> String {
230	    if let Some(pos) = s.find('\n') {
231	        format!("\x1b[1m{}\x1b[0m{}", &s[..pos], &s[pos..])
232	    } else {
233	        bold(s)
234	    }
235	}
236	
237	/// Indent all lines after the first by `n` spaces.
238	///
239	/// Returns the string unchanged if `n == 0` or there is only one line.
240	pub fn indent_body(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
241	    if n == 0 {
242	        return s.to_string();
243	    }
244	    let pad = " ".repeat(n);
245	    let mut lines = s.lines();
246	    let mut result = match lines.next() {
247	        Some(first) => first.to_string(),
248	        None => return String::new(),
249	    };
250	    for line in lines {
251	        result.push('\n');
252	        if !line.is_empty() {
253	            result.push_str(&pad);
254	        }
255	        result.push_str(line);
256	    }
257	    result
258	}
259	
260	/// Extract the ochid trailer value from a commit description, if present.
261	pub fn extract_ochid(commit: &Commit) -> Option<String> {
262	    for line in commit.description().lines().rev() {
263	        let trimmed = line.trim();
264	        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
265	            return Some(value.trim().to_string());
266	        }
267	    }
268	    None
269	}

271	/// Local bookmark names pointing exactly at `commit_id`, space-separated.
272	///
273	/// Returns an empty string when no local bookmark targets the commit. Remote
274	/// bookmarks are not included — that's a separate display concern.
275	pub fn format_bookmarks_at(repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>, commit_id: &CommitId) -> String {
276	    let view = repo.view();
277	    let names: Vec<String> = view
278	        .local_bookmarks_for_commit(commit_id)
279	        .map(|(name, _)| name.as_str().to_string())
280	        .collect();
281	    names.join(" ")
282	}

284	/// Format: changeID ochid(padded) [bookmarks |] first-line.
285	///
286	/// ochid column is left-padded to `width` characters. If no ochid, a blank
287	/// placeholder of that width is used. When `bookmarks` is non-empty it is
288	/// inserted before the title with a `|` separator (mirroring jj's
289	/// `Parent commit (@-): <chid> <hash> <bookmark> | <title>` style).
290	pub fn format_commit_with_ochid(commit: &Commit, width: usize, bookmarks: &str) -> String {
291	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
292	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
293	    let ochid = extract_ochid(commit).unwrap_or_default(); // OK: no ochid trailer → empty string
294	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
295	    let title = if first_line.is_empty() {
296	        "(no description set)"
297	    } else {
298	        first_line
299	    };
300	    if bookmarks.is_empty() {
301	        format!("{change_short}  {ochid:<width$}  {title}")
302	    } else {
303	        format!("{change_short}  {ochid:<width$}  {bookmarks} | {title}")
304	    }
305	}
306	
307	/// Format: just the short changeID.
308	pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
309	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
310	    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
311	}
312	
313	/// Format: changeID commitID [bookmarks |] first-line (single line).
314	///
315	/// When `bookmarks` is non-empty it is inserted before the title with a `|`
316	/// separator (mirroring jj's `<chid> <hash> <bookmark> | <title>` style).
317	pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit, bookmarks: &str) -> String {
318	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
319	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
320	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
321	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
322	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
323	    let title = if first_line.is_empty() {
324	        "(no description set)"
325	    } else {
326	        first_line
327	    };
328	    if bookmarks.is_empty() {
329	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {title}")
330	    } else {
331	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {bookmarks} | {title}")
332	    }
333	}
334	
335	/// Format: changeID commitID [bookmarks |] first-line, then remaining description lines.
336	///
337	/// Same header rules as `format_commit_short`; body lines follow unchanged.
338	pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit, bookmarks: &str) -> String {
339	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
340	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
341	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
342	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
343	    let desc = commit.description();
344	    if desc.is_empty() {
345	        return if bookmarks.is_empty() {
346	            format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
347	        } else {
348	            format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {bookmarks} | (no description set)")
349	        };
350	    }
351	    let mut lines = desc.lines();
352	    let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
353	    let mut result = if bookmarks.is_empty() {
354	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
355	    } else {
356	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {bookmarks} | {first_line}")
357	    };
358	    for line in lines {
359	        result.push('\n');
360	        result.push_str(line);
361	    }
362	    result
363	}
364	
365	/// Collect commit IDs for `..x..` (both directions) or any dot notation.
366	///
367	/// Returns `(commit_ids, anchor_index)` — the IDs in display order
368	/// (descendants closest to anchor, anchor, ancestors closest to anchor)
369	/// and the index of the anchor commit within the list.
370	///
371	/// - `desc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest descendants, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
372	/// - `anc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest ancestors, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
373	pub fn collect_ids(
374	    workspace: &Workspace,
375	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
376	    rev: &str,
377	    desc_count: Option<usize>,
378	    anc_count: Option<usize>,
379	) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
380	    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, rev)?;
381	    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
382	        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{rev}'").into());
383	    }
384	    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
385	    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
386	
387	    // Descendants (closest to anchor)
388	    let mut desc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
389	    if desc_count != Some(0) {
390	        let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("{rev}::"))?;
391	        desc_ids = descendant_ids
392	            .into_iter()
393	            .filter(|id| *id != root_commit_id && *id != *anchor_id)
394	            .collect();
395	        if let Some(n) = desc_count {
396	            let start = desc_ids.len().saturating_sub(n);
397	            desc_ids = desc_ids[start..].to_vec();
398	        }
399	    }
400	
401	    // Ancestors (closest to anchor)
402	    let mut anc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
403	    if anc_count != Some(0) {
404	        let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("::{rev}"))?;
405	        let limit = anc_count.unwrap_or(usize::MAX); // OK: no --ancestors limit → unbounded
406	        let mut count = 0;
407	        for commit_id in ancestor_ids {
408	            if commit_id == root_commit_id || commit_id == *anchor_id {
409	                continue;
410	            }
411	            anc_ids.push(commit_id);
412	            count += 1;
413	            if count >= limit {
414	                break;
415	            }
416	        }
417	    }
418	
419	    let anchor_index = desc_ids.len();
420	    let mut result = desc_ids;
421	    result.push(anchor_id.clone());
422	    result.extend(anc_ids);
423	    Ok((result, anchor_index))
424	}
425	
426	/// Header style between repos in multi-repo output.
427	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
428	pub enum Header {
429	    /// Show `deco path deco` header per repo.
430	    Label(String),
431	    /// No header at all.
432	    None,
433	}
434	
435	/// Resolve `-l` / `-L` flags into a `Header`.
436	///
437	/// `-L` (no header) takes precedence over `-l` (label decoration).
438	pub fn resolve_header(label: &str, suppress: bool) -> Header {
439	    if suppress {
440	        Header::None
441	    } else {
442	        Header::Label(label.to_string())
443	    }
444	}
445	
446	/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `["."]`, and run a closure for each.
447	///
448	/// Header style controls output between repos.
449	/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.
450	pub fn for_each_repo<F>(
451	    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],
452	    header: &Header,
453	    mut body: F,
454	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
455	where
456	    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,
457	{
458	    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {
459	        vec![PathBuf::from(".")]
460	    } else {
461	        raw_repos
462	            .iter()
463	            .flat_map(|p| {
464	                p.to_string_lossy()
465	                    .split(',')
466	                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))
467	                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
468	            })
469	            .collect()
470	    };
471	    let multi = repos.len() > 1;
472	    let mut first = true;
473	    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
474	
475	    for repo_path in &repos {
476	        if multi {
477	            match header {
478	                Header::Label(deco) => {
479	                    if !first {
480	                        info!("");
481	                    }
482	                    info!(
483	                        "{}",
484	                        bold(&format!("{deco} {} {deco}", repo_path.display()))
485	                    );
486	                }
487	                Header::None => {}
488	            }
489	        }
490	        first = false;
491	
492	        match load_repo(repo_path) {
493	            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {
494	                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {
495	                    error!("({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
496	                    errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
497	                }
498	            }
499	            Err(e) => {
500	                error!("({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
501	                errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
502	            }
503	        }
504	    }
505	
506	    if errors.is_empty() {
507	        Ok(())
508	    } else {
509	        Err(format!("{} repo(s) had errors", errors.len()).into())
510	    }
511	}
512	
513	pub fn load_repo(
514	    path: &Path,
515	) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
516	    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
517	    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
518	    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
519	    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
520	
521	    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
522	    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
523	
524	    Ok((workspace, repo))
525	}
526	
527	/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
528	pub fn resolve_revset(
529	    workspace: &Workspace,
530	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
531	    revset_str: &str,
532	) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
533	    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
534	    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
535	    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
536	    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
537	    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
538	        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
539	        base: workspace_root,
540	    };
541	    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
542	    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
543	        path_converter: &path_converter,
544	        workspace_name,
545	    };
546	    let context = RevsetParseContext {
547	        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
548	        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
549	        user_email: "",
550	        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
551	        default_ignored_remote: None,
552	        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
553	        use_glob_by_default: false,
554	        extensions: &extensions,
555	        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
556	    };
557	
558	    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
559	    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
560	
561	    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
562	    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
563	    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
564	    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
565	
566	    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
567	    for result in revset.iter() {
568	        commit_ids.push(result?);
569	    }
570	    Ok(commit_ids)
571	}
572	
573	/// Scan `jj bookmark list -a <name>` output for non-tracking remote refs.
574	///
575	/// Tracked remotes appear indented (`  @origin: ...`); non-tracking remotes
576	/// appear at column 0 as `<bookmark>@<remote>: ...`. Returns the
577	/// non-tracking remote name if any is found.
578	pub fn find_non_tracking_remote(list_output: &str, bookmark: &str) -> Option<String> {
579	    let prefix = format!("{bookmark}@");
580	    for line in list_output.lines() {
581	        if line.starts_with(&prefix)
582	            && let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(&prefix)
583	            && let Some((remote, _)) = rest.split_once(':')
584	        {
585	            return Some(remote.to_string());
586	        }
587	    }
588	    None
589	}
590	
591	/// Verify all remote refs for `bookmark` in `repo` are tracked.
592	///
593	/// Returns `Err` with the exact `jj bookmark track …` remediation command if
594	/// any non-tracking remote ref is found. Used as a preflight by repo-modifying
595	/// commands (sync, push, finalize) and as a post-condition assertion by setup
596	/// commands (init, clone, test-fixture).
597	pub fn verify_tracking(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
598	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
599	    let cwd = Path::new(".");
600	    let all = run(
601	        "jj",
602	        &["bookmark", "list", "-a", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str],
603	        cwd,
604	    )?;
605	    if let Some(remote) = find_non_tracking_remote(&all, bookmark) {
606	        return Err(format!(
607	            "bookmark '{bookmark}' has non-tracking remote '{bookmark}@{remote}' — \
608	             run `jj bookmark track {bookmark} --remote={remote} -R {repo_str}` to fix"
609	        )
610	        .into());
611	    }
612	    Ok(())
613	}
614
```

```
1	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
2	use std::process::Command;
3	
4	use clap::Args;
5	use log::{LevelFilter, debug, info, warn};
6	
7	use crate::common::run;
8	
9	/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.
10	///
11	/// Default is `--check`: fetch + classify + report, **error** if any
12	/// repo is `behind` or `diverged`. Pass `--no-check` to actually
13	/// rebase / fast-forward. On any failure the starting state of every
14	/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.
15	///
16	/// Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`
17	/// explicitly rather than relying on the default — defaults can shift,
18	/// explicit flags lock in the contract.
19	///
20	/// Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and
21	/// `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo projects
22	/// (e.g. `vc-template-x1`) or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces.
23	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
24	pub struct SyncArgs {
25	    /// Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action.
26	    /// This is the default; pass explicitly in scripts.
27	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "no_check")]
28	    pub check: bool,
29	
30	    /// Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed.
31	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "check")]
32	    pub no_check: bool,
33	
34	    /// Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)
35	    #[arg(short, long)]
36	    pub quiet: bool,
37	
38	    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo
39	    #[arg(long, default_value = "main")]
40	    pub bookmark: String,
41	
42	    /// Remote to sync against
43	    #[arg(long, default_value = "origin")]
44	    pub remote: String,
45	
46	    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated [default: `.,.claude`]
47	    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
48	    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
49	}
50	
51	/// Default repo set when `-R` isn't given — the canonical dual-repo pair.
52	const DEFAULT_REPOS: [&str; 2] = [".", ".claude"];
53	
54	/// Resolve the caller's `-R` flags into a concrete repo list.
55	///
56	/// Empty input (no `-R` given) falls back to `DEFAULT_REPOS`. Each
57	/// provided value is also split on `,` and trimmed, so
58	/// `-R .,.claude` works identically to `-R . -R .claude`.
59	fn resolve_repos(raw: &[PathBuf]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
60	    if raw.is_empty() {
61	        return DEFAULT_REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();
62	    }
63	    raw.iter()
64	        .flat_map(|p| {
65	            p.to_string_lossy()
66	                .split(',')
67	                .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))
68	                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
69	        })
70	        .collect()
71	}
72	
73	/// Relationship between a local bookmark and its remote counterpart.
74	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
75	pub enum State {
76	    /// Local and remote point at the same commit.
77	    UpToDate,
78	    /// Local is a strict ancestor of remote — fast-forward possible.
79	    Behind { local: String, remote: String },
80	    /// Remote is a strict ancestor of local — nothing to pull in.
81	    Ahead { local: String, remote: String },
82	    /// Neither is an ancestor of the other — needs rebase.
83	    Diverged { local: String, remote: String },
84	    /// The bookmark has no `@<remote>` counterpart.
85	    NoRemote,
86	}
87	
88	/// Per-repo context accumulated between the snapshot and action phases.
89	#[derive(Debug)]
90	struct RepoCtx {
91	    path: PathBuf,
92	    #[allow(dead_code)]
93	    op_id: String,
94	    state: State,
95	}
96	
97	/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.
98	///
99	/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that resolves the `-R` flag into a
100	/// concrete repo list (falling back to the dual-repo default) and
101	/// forwards the rest of the args. Tests call `sync_repos` directly
102	/// with absolute fixture paths.
103	///
104	/// When `--quiet` is set, the global log filter is temporarily clamped
105	/// to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on return, so
106	/// `info!` calls throughout sync (plus any subprocess-stderr routed
107	/// through `common::run`) go dark. Errors still surface at `Warn` /
108	/// `Error` so script callers don't lose diagnostics.
109	pub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
110	    let repos = resolve_repos(&args.repos);
111	    if args.quiet {
112	        let prev = log::max_level();
113	        log::set_max_level(LevelFilter::Warn);
114	        let result = sync_repos(&repos, args);
115	        log::set_max_level(prev);
116	        result
117	    } else {
118	        sync_repos(&repos, args)
119	    }
120	}
```

```
1	use std::path::PathBuf;
2	use std::process::Stdio;
3	use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
4	
5	use clap::Args;
6	use log::{debug, info};

8	use crate::common::run;
9	
10	/// Directory where the detached finalize child writes failure markers.
11	/// A subsequent `vc-x1` invocation scans this directory and surfaces
12	/// any failures to the user, closing the gap that the detached child's
13	/// non-zero exit isn't observable by the caller.
14	fn status_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
15	    std::env::var_os("HOME").map(|h| PathBuf::from(h).join(".cache/vc-x1/finalize-status"))
16	}
17	
18	fn write_failure_marker(opts: &FinalizeOpts, err: &str) {
19	    let Some(dir) = status_dir() else {
20	        return;
21	    };
22	    if std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).is_err() {
23	        return;
24	    }
25	    let ns = SystemTime::now()
26	        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
27	        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
28	        .unwrap_or(0); // OK: filesystem-sortable timestamp; 0 on the impossible pre-epoch path
29	    let pid = std::process::id();
30	    let path = dir.join(format!("{ns}-{pid}.status"));
31	    let content = format!(
32	        "timestamp_ns={ns}\npid={pid}\nrepo={}\nbookmark={}\nerror={err}\n",
33	        opts.repo.display(),
34	        opts.bookmark.as_deref().unwrap_or(""),
35	    );
36	    let _ = std::fs::write(&path, content);
37	}
38	
39	/// Read, print, and delete any failure markers left by previous detached
40	/// finalize children. Cheap no-op when the directory doesn't exist.
41	pub fn surface_previous_failures() {
42	    let Some(dir) = status_dir() else {
43	        return;
44	    };
45	    let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(&dir) else {
46	        return;
47	    };
48	    let mut paths: Vec<PathBuf> = entries
49	        .flatten()
50	        .map(|e| e.path())
51	        .filter(|p| p.extension().and_then(|s| s.to_str()) == Some("status"))
52	        .collect();
53	    paths.sort();
54	    for path in paths {
55	        if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
56	            eprintln!(
57	                "warn: previous finalize failure ({}):",
58	                path.file_stem()
59	                    .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy())
60	                    .unwrap_or_default() // OK: filename without extension; empty on unexpected absence
61	            );
62	            for line in content.lines() {
63	                eprintln!("  {line}");
64	            }
65	            let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
66	        }
67	    }
68	}
69	
70	/// Squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo.
71	///
72	/// Designed for the bot to atomically finalize its session repo:
73	/// --detach exits immediately, --delay waits for trailing writes,
74	/// --squash folds them in, --bookmark + --push sends it upstream.
75	/// Every flag is opt-in. See README.md for details.
76	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
77	pub struct FinalizeArgs {
78	    /// Path to jj repo
79	    #[arg(long, default_value = ".")]
80	    pub repo: PathBuf,
81	
82	    /// Squash SOURCE into TARGET [default: @,@-]
83	    #[arg(long, value_name = "SOURCE,TARGET", default_missing_value = "@,@-", num_args = 0..=1)]
84	    pub squash: Option<String>,
85	
86	    /// Seconds to wait before squashing
87	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0)]
88	    pub delay: f64,
89	
90	    /// Existing bookmark to push to remote
91	    #[arg(long, value_name = "BOOKMARK")]
92	    pub push: Option<String>,
93	
94	    /// Detach and run in the background
95	    #[arg(long)]
96	    pub detach: bool,
97	
98	    /// Internal: run exec path (used by detach)
99	    #[arg(long, hide = true)]
100	    pub exec: bool,
```

> AGENT

I've read the design (chores-06 `[60]` + `[65]`), the workspace-state invariant (three states: POR / single-repo / dual-repo), the existing `-R`/`--repo`/`--scope`-shaped surfaces, and how each command currently assumes dual-repo today. Here's what I'd propose — I want your approval on shape + open questions before writing any code.

## Current assumptions to dismantle

- `sync`: `DEFAULT_REPOS = [".", ".claude"]` hard-coded in `sync.rs:52`.
- `push`: always dual; `finalize-claude` stage hard-codes `.claude` path in `push.rs:1202/1207/1213`.
- `finalize`: already single-repo per invocation (`--repo`), so it's fine semantically; naming needs rationalization.
- `main.rs bm_track`: already walks up for `.vc-config.toml` with `path="/"` — most of the workspace-root detection is there but limited to dual-repo assumption (`repos` array hard-codes `.claude`).
- `.vc-config.toml`: already has `path = "/"` and `other-repo = ".claude"` — no schema change needed for this cycle.

## Proposed cycle: `0.40.0-N`, five steps

Multi-step, not single-step — this is foundational and exploratory (scope-resolution semantics deserve their own commits; retrofits per command are each discretely testable).

- **0.40.0-0** — foundation, no behavior change. Add `common::workspace` module: `WorkspaceState` enum (`Por`, `SingleRepo{root}`, `DualRepo{root, other}`), `detect()` walker, `ScopeArg` flag type (`#[derive(Args)]`, values `app|other|both`), and `resolve(state, scope) → Vec<PathBuf>` enforcing the three-state policy per `[65]`. Unit tests. Move `main::find_workspace_root` into the new module.
- **0.40.0-1** — retrofit `sync`. Add `--scope`; default becomes "resolve via workspace state" (POR/single → `[.]`, dual → `[., other]`). Keep `-R` working unchanged (no deprecation this cycle). Mutual-exclude `--scope` and `-R` via clap.
- **0.40.0-2** — retrofit `push`. Add `--scope`. Use `workspace.other` instead of literal `.claude`. `--scope=app` skips `commit-claude` + `finalize-claude` stages. Refuse `other`/`both` outside dual. Update state file shape only if necessary (prefer additive).
- **0.40.0-3** — retrofit `finalize`. Keep `--repo` as the primary path input (it already works everywhere); add `--scope` as an alternative that resolves via workspace state. Update `main::bm_track` to use `WorkspaceState` instead of its hard-coded `.claude` probe.
- **0.40.0** — release: `notes/todo.md` Done, `notes/chores-06.md` subsection, CLAUDE.md refresh if new vocabulary surfaces, README updates for the three affected commands.

## Deliberately out of scope for this cycle

- `other-repo` → `counterpart` config rename ([65] suggestion). Add as a follow-up todo.
- Removing/deprecating `-R`. Design said "alias for one minor cycle, deprecate, remove." This cycle = the alias window; deprecation warning lands in 0.41.0.
- Retrofitting read-only commands (chid/desc/list/show/validate-desc/fix-desc). They already take `-R` and are multi-repo-flexible. A `--scope` convenience can be added later; not required for the "operate on any repo" goal.

## Open questions

1. **Scope default when `--scope` omitted on retrofitted commands:** I assumed "workspace-state driven" (POR/single → app, dual → both). Alternative is "always both, error if not dual" — more explicit but noisier in single-repo workspaces. Prefer state-driven?
2. **`-R` + `--scope` together:** clap mutual-exclusion (error) or silent precedence (one wins)? I lean error — catches confusion early.
3. **Single-step vs multi-step:** proposing multi-step `0.40.0-N`. Switch to single-step `0.40.0` if you'd rather one larger commit?

Let me know and I'll start with step 0.

 - All other commands it's an error

 - The "plan" is always subject to change as we learn

Here's the proposed chores-06 subsection for step `-0`. Nothing else changes in `-0` besides `Cargo.toml` bump + `todo.md` Todo→In Progress move.

---

**Proposed `notes/chores-06.md` new section (at end of file):**

```markdown
## Generalize --scope across commands (0.40.0)

Foundation for item #1 on todo ([60]): commands should support
single-repo, dual-repo, and plain-old-repo (POR) workspaces
without hard-coding the `.claude` dual-repo assumption.

**Scope default resolution** (no `--scope` given):

- No `.vc-config.toml` → `--scope=app`.
- `.vc-config.toml` present but `other-repo` missing or empty
  → `--scope=app`.
- `.vc-config.toml` with a non-empty `other-repo` → `--scope=both`.

**Ambiguity rule.** Any ambiguous combination of scope-related
flags on a single invocation (e.g. `-R` with `--scope`) is a
fatal error — callers must pick one way to express repo
selection per call.

**Cycle steps.**

- **0.40.0-0** — this plan. Notes + version bump only.
- **0.40.0-1** — `vc-x1 init --scope=app|other|both`.
  - `app` → single-repo workspace (`.vc-config.toml` with
    `path = "/"` and no `other-repo`; no `.claude` subdir; one
    GitHub repo; no `ochid:` trailer on the initial commit).
  - `both` (default) → current dual-repo behavior.
  - `other` → error; meaningless at init time.
  - Every other subcommand errors loudly if `--scope` is passed.
- Subsequent `-N` steps wire `--scope` into sync, push,
  finalize, and the read-only commands. Specifics decided when
  each lands.

**Helper placement is deliberately open** — `common.rs`, `init.rs`,
or a dedicated `scope.rs` / `scope_helpers.rs` are all fine for
step `-1`; we can refactor later once more call sites exist. The
plan is subject to change as we learn; `-0` records the current
best guess, not a contract.
```

**Plus `notes/todo.md`:** move the `--scope` generalization item from `## Todo` to `## In Progress`, and add a new reference `[70]: /notes/chores-06.md#generalize---scope-across-commands-0400`.

**Plus `Cargo.toml`:** `version = "0.40.0-0"`.

## Step `-0` file list

1. `Cargo.toml` — version bump.
2. `Cargo.lock` — regenerates on `cargo check`.
3. `notes/chores-06.md` — append the section above.
4. `notes/todo.md` — In Progress move + ref `[70]`.

No code, no tests, no README. Pre-commit checklist is shortened: `cargo fmt` and `cargo check` (fmt touches nothing meaningful here; check confirms `Cargo.lock` regenerates cleanly). No clippy/test delta expected since no `.rs` changes.

OK to proceed with these edits?

Notes + version bump only.\n- **0.40.0-1** — `vc-x1 init --scope=app|other|both`.\n  - `app` → single-repo workspace (`.vc-config.toml` with\n    `path = \"/\"` and no `other-repo`; no `.claude` subdir; one\n    GitHub repo; no `ochid:` trailer on the initial commit).\n  - `both` (default) → current dual-repo behavior.\n  - `other` → error; meaningless at init time.\n  - Every other subcommand errors loudly if `--scope` is passed.\n- Subsequent `-N` steps wire `--scope` into sync, push,\n  finalize, and the read-only commands. Specifics decided when\n  each lands.\n\n**Helper placement is deliberately open** — `common.rs`,\n`init.rs`, or a dedicated `scope.rs` / `scope_helpers.rs` are\nall fine for step `-1`; we can refactor later once more call\nsites exist. The plan is subject to change as we learn; `-0`\nrecords the current best guess, not a contract.\n\n# References"
```json
}
```

```
42	    /// not nested). Non-hidden contents are copied recursively; hidden
43	    /// entries (names starting with `.`) are skipped since init creates the

49	
50	/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
51	fn run_retry(
52	    cmd: &str,
53	    args: &[&str],
54	    cwd: &Path,
55	    retries: u32,
56	    delay_secs: u64,
57	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
58	    let mut last_err = String::new();
59	    for attempt in 1..=retries {
60	        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {
61	            Ok(out) => {
62	                if attempt > 1 {
63	                    debug!("succeeded after {attempt} attempts");
64	                }
65	                return Ok(out);
66	            }
67	            Err(e) => {
68	                last_err = e.to_string();
69	                if attempt < retries {
70	                    debug!("attempt {attempt}/{retries} failed: {last_err}");
71	                    debug!("retrying in {delay_secs}s...");
72	                    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(delay_secs));
73	                }
74	            }
75	        }
76	    }
77	    Err(format!("failed after {retries} attempts: {last_err}").into())
78	}
79	
80	use crate::common::{mkdir_p, run, write_file};
81	
82	/// Parse the `--use-template` value into `(code, bot)` template paths.
83	///
84	/// Format: `CODE[,BOT]`. If `BOT` is omitted, the default is the sibling
85	/// directory whose name is `<CODE-basename>.claude` (via
86	/// `Path::with_file_name`, so a trailing slash on `CODE` does not produce
87	/// a different result).
88	pub(crate) fn parse_use_template(
89	    s: &str,
90	) -> Result<(PathBuf, PathBuf), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
91	    let mut parts = s.splitn(2, ',');
92	    let code_raw = parts.next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: splitn always yields at least one element
93	    let bot_raw = parts.next();
94	    let code_trim = code_raw.trim();
95	    if code_trim.is_empty() {
96	        return Err("--use-template: code template path is empty".into());
97	    }
98	    let code = PathBuf::from(code_trim);
99	    let bot = match bot_raw.map(str::trim) {
100	        Some(b) if !b.is_empty() => PathBuf::from(b),
101	        _ => {
102	            let file_name = code.file_name().ok_or_else(|| {
103	                format!(
104	                    "--use-template: cannot derive default bot path from '{}' (no file name component)",
105	                    code.display()
106	                )
107	            })?;
108	            let new_name = format!("{}.claude", file_name.to_string_lossy());
109	            code.with_file_name(new_name)
110	        }
111	    };
112	    Ok((code, bot))
113	}
114	
115	/// Top-level non-hidden files init writes. Kept here so that if init is
116	/// ever extended to write non-hidden top-level files, the pre-flight
117	/// conflict scan flags any template that would clash. Currently empty
118	/// because init only writes hidden files (`.vc-config.toml`, `.gitignore`),
119	/// and the template copy skips hidden entries.
120	const RESERVED_TEMPLATE_ENTRIES: &[&str] = &[];
121	
122	/// Validate that both template paths exist, are directories, and contain no
123	/// top-level non-hidden entry that would collide with a file init writes.
124	pub(crate) fn validate_templates(
125	    code: &Path,
126	    bot: &Path,
127	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
128	    for (label, p) in [("code", code), ("bot", bot)] {
129	        if !p.exists() {
130	            return Err(format!(
131	                "--use-template: {label} template '{}' does not exist",
132	                p.display()
133	            )
134	            .into());
135	        }
136	        if !p.is_dir() {
137	            return Err(format!(
138	                "--use-template: {label} template '{}' is not a directory",
139	                p.display()
140	            )
141	            .into());
142	        }
143	        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(p)? {
144	            let entry = entry?;
145	            let name = entry.file_name();
146	            let name_str = name.to_string_lossy();
147	            if name_str.starts_with('.') {
148	                continue;
149	            }
150	            if RESERVED_TEMPLATE_ENTRIES.contains(&name_str.as_ref()) {
151	                return Err(format!(
152	                    "--use-template: {label} template '{}' contains reserved entry '{}'",
153	                    p.display(),
154	                    name_str
155	                )
156	                .into());
157	            }
158	        }
159	    }
160	    Ok(())
161	}
162	
163	/// Recursively copy non-hidden entries from `src` to `dst`. Any entry whose
164	/// file name starts with `.` is skipped. Symlinks are skipped with a debug
165	/// log — templates don't need them, and following them risks escaping the
166	/// template tree.
167	pub(crate) fn copy_template_recursive(
168	    src: &Path,
169	    dst: &Path,
170	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
171	    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(src)? {
172	        let entry = entry?;
173	        let name = entry.file_name();
174	        let name_str = name.to_string_lossy();
175	        if name_str.starts_with('.') {
176	            continue;
177	        }
178	        let src_path = entry.path();
179	        let dst_path = dst.join(&name);
180	        let ft = entry.file_type()?;
181	        if ft.is_symlink() {
182	            debug!("skip symlink {}", src_path.display());
183	            continue;
184	        }
185	        if ft.is_dir() {
186	            mkdir_p(&dst_path)?;
187	            copy_template_recursive(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
188	        } else if ft.is_file() {
189	            debug!("copy {} -> {}", src_path.display(), dst_path.display());
190	            std::fs::copy(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
191	        }
192	    }
193	    Ok(())
194	}
195	
196	/// Replace the first line of `<dir>/README.md` with `# <name>`. If
197	/// `README.md` is absent, this is a no-op. Trailing content after the
198	/// first newline is preserved verbatim; a file with no newline becomes
199	/// just `# <name>`.
200	pub(crate) fn rewrite_readme_first_line(
201	    dir: &Path,
202	    name: &str,
203	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
204	    let readme = dir.join("README.md");
205	    if !readme.exists() {
206	        return Ok(());
207	    }
208	    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&readme)?;
209	    let rest = match content.find('\n') {
210	        Some(pos) => &content[pos..],
211	        None => "",
212	    };
213	    let new_content = format!("# {name}{rest}");
214	    std::fs::write(&readme, new_content)?;
215	    debug!("rewrote first line of {}", readme.display());
216	    Ok(())
217	}
218	
219	/// Get the short (12-char) jj change ID for a revision, without printing.
220	fn jj_chid(rev: &str, cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
221	    let full = run(
222	        "jj",
223	        &[
224	            "log",
225	            "-r",
226	            rev,
227	            "--no-graph",
228	            "-T",
229	            "change_id",
230	            "--limit",
231	            "1",
232	        ],
233	        cwd,
234	    )?;
235	    Ok(full[..full.len().min(12)].to_string())
236	}

241	}

245	    let full = format!("{owner}/{name}");
246	    Ok(run("gh", &["repo", "view", &full], Path::new(".")).is_ok())
247	}
```
