282	pub fn init(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
283	    debug!("init: enter");
284	    let parent_dir = match &args.dir {
285	        Some(d) => d.clone(),
286	        None => std::env::current_dir()?,
287	    };
288	    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&args.name);
289	    let session_dir = project_dir.join(".claude");
290	
291	    // Preflight checks
292	    info!("Preflight checks...");
293	
294	    // Check tools
295	    run("gh", &["auth", "status"], Path::new("."))
296	        .map_err(|_| "gh is not installed or not authenticated (run: gh auth login)")?;
297	    run("jj", &["--version"], Path::new(".")).map_err(|_| "jj is not installed")?;
298	
299	    let owner = match &args.owner {
300	        Some(o) => o.clone(),
301	        None => gh_whoami()?,
302	    };
303	
304	    if project_dir.exists() {
305	        return Err(format!("'{}' already exists", project_dir.display()).into());
306	    }
307	
308	    let code_repo = format!("{owner}/{}", args.name);
309	    let session_repo = format!("{owner}/{}.claude", args.name);
310	
311	    if gh_repo_exists(&owner, &args.name)? {
312	        return Err(format!("GitHub repo '{code_repo}' already exists").into());
313	    }
314	    if gh_repo_exists(&owner, &format!("{}.claude", args.name))? {
315	        return Err(format!("GitHub repo '{session_repo}' already exists").into());
316	    }
317	
318	    let templates = match &args.use_template {
319	        Some(s) => {
320	            let (code_t, bot_t) = parse_use_template(s)?;
321	            validate_templates(&code_t, &bot_t)?;
322	            Some((code_t, bot_t))
323	        }
324	        None => None,
325	    };
326	
327	    let visibility = if args.private {
328	        "--private"
329	    } else {
330	        "--public"
331	    };
332	
333	    if args.dry_run {
334	        info!("Dry run — would execute:");
335	        info!("  1. Create directories: {}", project_dir.display());
336	        info!("  2. git init + jj git init --colocate on both repos");
337	        info!("  3. Write .vc-config.toml and .gitignore to both");
338	        match &templates {
339	            Some((c, b)) => {
340	                info!(
341	                    "  4. Copy templates (non-hidden) into both repos + rewrite README.md first line"
342	                );
343	                info!("       code: {}", c.display());
344	                info!("       bot:  {}", b.display());
345	            }
346	            None => info!("  4. (skipped — no --use-template)"),
347	        }
348	        info!("  5. jj commit both with placeholder ochids");
349	        info!("  6. Get both chids, jj describe both with correct ochids");
350	        info!("  7. Remove jj from both (git clean -xdf)");
351	        info!("  8. gh repo create {session_repo} {visibility}, push");
352	        info!("  9. gh repo create {code_repo} {visibility}, push");
353	        info!("  10. jj git init --colocate on both repos");
354	        info!("  11. Create Claude Code symlink");
355	        return Ok(());
356	    }
357	
358	    // Step 1: Create directories
359	    info!("Step 1: Creating project directories...");
360	    mkdir_p(&project_dir)?;
361	    mkdir_p(&session_dir)?;
362	
363	    // Step 2: git init + jj init on both repos
364	    info!("Step 2: Initializing repos...");
365	    run("git", &["init"], &project_dir)?;
366	    run("jj", &["git", "init", "--colocate"], &project_dir)?;
367	    run("git", &["init"], &session_dir)?;
368	    run("jj", &["git", "init", "--colocate"], &session_dir)?;
369	
370	    // Step 3: Write config files
371	    info!("Step 3: Writing config files...");
372	    write_file(&project_dir.join(".vc-config.toml"), VC_CONFIG_CODE)?;
373	    write_file(&project_dir.join(".gitignore"), GITIGNORE_CODE)?;
374	    write_file(&session_dir.join(".vc-config.toml"), VC_CONFIG_SESSION)?;
375	    write_file(&session_dir.join(".gitignore"), GITIGNORE_SESSION)?;
376	
377	    // Step 4: Copy templates (if --use-template)
378	    if let Some((code_t, bot_t)) = &templates {
379	        info!("Step 4: Copying templates...");
380	        info!("  code: {} -> {}", code_t.display(), project_dir.display());
381	        copy_template_recursive(code_t, &project_dir)?;
382	        rewrite_readme_first_line(&project_dir, &args.name)?;
383	        info!("  bot:  {} -> {}", bot_t.display(), session_dir.display());
384	        copy_template_recursive(bot_t, &session_dir)?;
385	        let session_name = format!("{}.claude", args.name);
386	        rewrite_readme_first_line(&session_dir, &session_name)?;
387	    }
388	
389	    // Step 5: jj commit both with placeholder ochids
390	    info!("Step 5: Committing both repos with placeholder ochids...");
391	    run(
392	        "jj",
393	        &["commit", "-m", "Initial commit\n\nochid: /none"],
394	        &project_dir,
395	    )?;
396	    run(
397	        "jj",
398	        &["commit", "-m", "Initial commit\n\nochid: /none"],
399	        &session_dir,
400	    )?;
401	
402	    // Step 6: Get both chids, then describe both with correct ochids
403	    info!("Step 6: Setting ochid cross-references...");
404	    let code_chid = jj_chid("@-", &project_dir)?;
405	    let session_chid = jj_chid("@-", &session_dir)?;
406	
407	    let code_desc = format!("Initial commit\n\nochid: /.claude/{session_chid}");
408	    let session_desc = format!("Initial commit\n\nochid: /{code_chid}");
409	    run("jj", &["describe", "@-", "-m", &code_desc], &project_dir)?;
410	    run("jj", &["describe", "@-", "-m", &session_desc], &session_dir)?;
411	
412	    {
413	        let hash = run("git", &["rev-parse", "HEAD"], &project_dir)?;
414	        debug!("code repo: chid={code_chid} hash={hash}");
415	        let hash = run("git", &["rev-parse", "HEAD"], &session_dir)?;
416	        debug!(".claude:   chid={session_chid} hash={hash}");
417	    }
418	
419	    // Step 7: Set bookmarks (creates git branches), then remove jj
420	    // Bookmarks must be set before removing .jj/ so git has a 'main' branch to push
421	    info!("Step 7: Setting bookmarks and removing jj...");
422	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], &project_dir)?;
423	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], &session_dir)?;
424	    // Clean .claude first, then code repo with --exclude to preserve .claude/
425	    run("git", &["clean", "-xdf"], &session_dir)?;
426	    run(
427	        "git",
428	        &["clean", "-xdf", "--exclude", ".claude"],
429	        &project_dir,
430	    )?;
431	    // After removing .jj/, git HEAD is detached — reattach to main
432	    run("git", &["checkout", "main"], &session_dir)?;
433	    run("git", &["checkout", "main"], &project_dir)?;
434	
435	    // Step 8: Create .claude GitHub repo and push
436	    let session_url = format!("git@github.com:{session_repo}.git");
437	    info!("Step 8: Creating GitHub repo {session_repo}...");
438	    run(
439	        "gh",
440	        &["repo", "create", &session_repo, visibility],
441	        &project_dir,
442	    )?;
443	    run(
444	        "git",
445	        &["remote", "add", "origin", &session_url],
446	        &session_dir,
447	    )?;
448	    run_retry(
449	        "git",
450	        &["push", "-u", "origin", "main"],
451	        &session_dir,
452	        args.push_retries,
453	        args.push_retry_delay,
454	    )?;
455	
456	    // Step 9: Create code GitHub repo and push
457	    let code_url = format!("git@github.com:{code_repo}.git");
458	    info!("Step 9: Creating GitHub repo {code_repo}...");
459	    run(
460	        "gh",
461	        &["repo", "create", &code_repo, visibility],
462	        &project_dir,
463	    )?;
464	    run("git", &["remote", "add", "origin", &code_url], &project_dir)?;
465	    run_retry(
466	        "git",
467	        &["push", "-u", "origin", "main"],
468	        &project_dir,
469	        args.push_retries,
470	        args.push_retry_delay,
471	    )?;
472	
473	    // Step 10: Re-initialize jj on both repos
474	    info!("Step 10: Re-initializing jj on both repos...");
475	    run(
476	        "jj",
477	        &["--quiet", "git", "init", "--colocate"],
478	        &project_dir,
479	    )?;
480	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], &project_dir)?;
481	    run(
482	        "jj",
483	        &["bookmark", "track", "main", "--remote=origin"],
484	        &project_dir,
485	    )?;
486	    crate::common::verify_tracking(&project_dir, "main")?;
487	    let code_chid_final = jj_chid("@-", &project_dir)?;
488	
489	    run(
490	        "jj",
491	        &["--quiet", "git", "init", "--colocate"],
492	        &session_dir,
493	    )?;
494	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], &session_dir)?;
495	    run(
496	        "jj",
497	        &["bookmark", "track", "main", "--remote=origin"],
498	        &session_dir,
499	    )?;
500	    crate::common::verify_tracking(&session_dir, "main")?;
501	    let session_chid_final = jj_chid("@-", &session_dir)?;
502	
503	    // Step 11: Create Claude Code symlink
504	    info!("Step 11: Creating Claude Code symlink...");
505	    let symlink_dir = {
506	        let home =
507	            std::env::var("HOME").map_err(|_| "HOME environment variable not set".to_string())?;
508	        PathBuf::from(home).join(".claude").join("projects")
509	    };
510	
511	    let sl = symlink::SymLink::new(&project_dir, Path::new(".claude"), &symlink_dir)?;
512	    sl.create(false)?;
513	
514	    info!("");
515	    info!("Done! Project created at {}", project_dir.display());
516	    info!("  Code repo:    {code_repo}  (chid={code_chid_final})");
517	    info!("  Session repo: {session_repo}  (chid={session_chid_final})");
518	    info!(
519	        "  Symlink:      {} -> {}",
520	        sl.symlink_path.display(),
521	        sl.abs_target.display()
522	    );
523	
524	    debug!("init: exit");
525	    Ok(())
526	}

671	    #[test]
672	    fn copy_template_skips_hidden_entries() {
673	        let root = tmp_root("copy-skip-hidden");
674	        let src = root.join("src");
675	        let dst = root.join("dst");
676	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&src).unwrap();
677	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dst).unwrap();
678	
679	        // Non-hidden: visible file, visible dir with nested file.
680	        std::fs::write(src.join("keep.txt"), "keep").unwrap();
681	        std::fs::create_dir_all(src.join("sub")).unwrap();
682	        std::fs::write(src.join("sub").join("nested.txt"), "nested").unwrap();
683	
684	        // Hidden: dotfile, dotdir (with contents that must NOT be copied).
685	        std::fs::write(src.join(".hidden"), "should-not-copy").unwrap();
686	        std::fs::create_dir_all(src.join(".dotdir")).unwrap();
687	        std::fs::write(src.join(".dotdir").join("inside"), "nope").unwrap();
688	
689	        copy_template_recursive(&src, &dst).unwrap();
690	
691	        assert_eq!(
692	            std::fs::read_to_string(dst.join("keep.txt")).unwrap(),
693	            "keep"
694	        );
695	        assert_eq!(
696	            std::fs::read_to_string(dst.join("sub").join("nested.txt")).unwrap(),
697	            "nested"
698	        );
699	        assert!(!dst.join(".hidden").exists());
700	        assert!(!dst.join(".dotdir").exists());
701	
702	        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
703	    }

## Step `-1` plan

**New file `src/scope.rs`** (small, ~20 lines):
- `Scope` enum: `App`, `Other`, `Both` with clap `ValueEnum` derive + brief module doc.

**`src/main.rs`:** add `mod scope;`.

**`src/init.rs`:**
- Add `scope: Option<Scope>` to `InitArgs` (default `None`; init resolves to `Both` to preserve current behavior — workspace doesn't exist yet at init time, so the "workspace-state-driven default" rule doesn't apply here).
- Add constants `VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY` (only `path = "/"`, no `other-repo`) and `GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY` (no `/.claude` line).
- Refactor `init()` into a small dispatcher + `init_app()` + `init_both()` (current flow renamed into `init_both` verbatim). Keeps the dual-repo path bit-identical to today.
- `init_app()` does the same 11-ish steps but: no session dir, no session `git init`/`jj init`, no second `.vc-config.toml`, no session template, no `ochid:` trailer on the initial commit (since there's no counterpart), single GitHub repo create+push, no symlink setup.
- `--scope=other` at init errors ("meaningless at init — no app repo to anchor to; use `--scope=app` or `--scope=both`").
- `--scope=app` + `--use-template CODE,BOT` errors per the ambiguity rule ("`--scope=app` takes a single template; got `CODE,BOT`").
- Tests: `--scope` arg parsing (all three values + unset), new constants' content, and the `--scope=app` + `,BOT` rejection.

**`notes/chores-06.md`:** append `### 0.40.0-1: vc-x1 init --scope` subsection under the existing umbrella, documenting what shipped.

**`Cargo.toml`:** bump to `0.40.0-1`.

## Other commands: how they "error"

No flag additions elsewhere. Clap natively rejects unknown args — `vc-x1 sync --scope=app` gives `error: unexpected argument '--scope' found`. Per your "all other commands it's an error" direction, that's sufficient for `-1`. Friendlier per-command errors can land when each command wires scope for real.

See\n//! `notes/chores-06.md > Generalize --scope across commands (0.40.0)`\n//! for the full design, including the default-resolution and\n//! ambiguity rules.\n\nuse clap::ValueEnum;\n\n/// Which repo(s) a command should operate on.\n#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]\npub enum Scope {\n    /// Operate only on the app repo (the workspace's `path = \"/\"` side).\n    App,\n    /// Operate only on the counterpart repo (via `other-repo` in\n    /// `.vc-config.toml`).\n    Other,\n    /// Operate on both repos together (dual-repo workspace only).\n    Both,\n}\n"
}

Non-hidden contents are copied recursively; hidden\n    /// entries (names starting with `.`) are skipped since init creates the\n    /// repo's own hidden files.   "new_string": "use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse log::{debug, info};\n\nuse crate::scope::Scope;\nuse crate::symlink;\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct InitArgs {\n    /// Project name (directory and GitHub repo name)\n    #[arg(value_name = \"NAME\")]\n    pub name: String,\n\n    /// GitHub user or organization\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub owner: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Parent directory to create project in [default: cwd]\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub dir: Option<PathBuf>,\n\n    /// Create private GitHub repos (default: public)\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub private: bool,\n\n    /// Dry run — show what would be done without executing\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub dry_run: bool,\n\n    /// Max push retries after repo creation [default: 5]\n    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 5)]\n    pub push_retries: u32,\n\n    /// Seconds between push retries [default: 3]\n    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 3)]\n    pub push_retry_delay: u64,\n\n    /// Seed repo(s) from template directories.\n    ///\n    /// Default (`--scope=both`): value is `CODE[,BOT]`; if `BOT` is\n    /// omitted, it defaults to the sibling directory `<CODE>.claude`\n    /// (file-name concat, not path join — they are not nested).\n    /// With `--scope=app`: value is a single `CODE` path; passing a\n    /// comma is a fatal error (no bot template applies).\n    /// Non-hidden contents are copied recursively; hidden entries\n    /// (names starting with `.`) are skipped since init creates the\n    /// repo's own hidden files. If the template has a `README.md`,\n    /// its first line is rewritten to `# <repo-name>`.\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"CODE[,BOT]\")]\n    pub use_template: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Workspace scope to initialize [default: both]\n    ///\n    /// `both` creates the dual-repo layout (app + `.claude`\n    /// session repo). `app` creates a single-repo workspace\n    /// (no `.claude`, no `other-repo` in config). `other` is\n    /// meaningless at init time and is a fatal error.\n    #[arg(long, value_enum, value_name = \"SCOPE\")]\n    pub scope: Option<Scope>,\n}"
}

No `other-repo`\n/// field — its absence is what downstream commands use to detect the\n/// single-repo state (per `notes/chores-06.md > Scope design\n/// refinements (0.37.8)`).\nconst VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY: &str = r#\"# vc-config: Vibe Coding workspace configuration\n#\n# workspace-path is this repo's path relative to the workspace root.\n# Used to resolve changeID paths in git trailers (e.g. ochid: /changeID).\n\n[workspace]\npath = \"/\"\n\"#;\n\nconst GITIGNORE_CODE: &str = \"/target\n/.claude\n/.git\n/.jj\n/.vc-x1\n\";\n\nconst GITIGNORE_SESSION: &str = \".git\n.jj\n\";\n\n/// Gitignore for a single-repo workspace — same as `GITIGNORE_CODE`\n/// minus the `/.claude` entry, since app-only workspaces never have a\n/// `.claude` subdir.\nconst GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY: &str = \"/target\n/.git\n/.jj\n/.vc-x1\n\";"

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/init.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn init(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"init: enter\");\n    let parent_dir = match &args.dir {\n        Some(d) => d.clone(),\n        None => std::env::current_dir()?,\n    };\n    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&args.name);\n    let session_dir = project_dir.join(\".claude\");",
  "new_string": "/// Dispatch on `--scope` (default `Both`): `Both` runs the full\n/// dual-repo init, `App` runs the single-repo variant, `Other` is a\n/// fatal error (no app repo to anchor to).\npub fn init(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match args.scope.unwrap_or(Scope::Both) {\n        Scope::Both => init_both(args),\n        Scope::App => init_app(args),\n        Scope::Other => Err(\n            \"--scope=other is meaningless at init time — use --scope=app or --scope=both\".into(),\n        ),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Dual-repo init — the original flow, creating both the app repo and\n/// the `.claude` session repo with cross-linked `ochid:` trailers.\nfn init_both(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"init: enter\");\n    let parent_dir = match &args.dir {\n        Some(d) => d.clone(),\n        None => std::env::current_dir()?,\n    };\n    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&args.name);\n    let session_dir = project_dir.join(\".claude\");"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "new_string": "    debug!(\"init: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Single-repo init — creates one jj/git repo, one GitHub repo, no\n/// `.claude` session, no `ochid:` trailer. Used when the caller\n/// passes `--scope=app` to signal \"plain-old-repo with vc-x1\n/// tooling, no companion.\"\nfn init_app(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"init_app: enter\");\n    let parent_dir = match &args.dir {\n        Some(d) => d.clone(),\n        None => std::env::current_dir()?,\n    };\n    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&args.name);\n\n    info!(\"Preflight checks...\");\n\n    run(\"gh\", &[\"auth\", \"status\"], Path::new(\".\"))\n        .map_err(|_| \"gh is not installed or not authenticated (run: gh auth login)\")?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"--version\"], Path::new(\".\")).map_err(|_| \"jj is not installed\")?;\n\n    let owner = match &args.owner {\n        Some(o) => o.clone(),\n        None => gh_whoami()?,\n    };\n\n    if project_dir.exists() {\n        return Err(format!(\"'{}' already exists\", project_dir.display()).into());\n    }\n\n    let code_repo = format!(\"{owner}/{}\", args.name);\n    if gh_repo_exists(&owner, &args.name)? {\n        return Err(format!(\"GitHub repo '{code_repo}' already exists\").into());\n    }\n\n    // In --scope=app, --use-template takes a single CODE path. A\n    // comma indicates dual-repo intent (CODE,BOT) — fatal per the\n    // ambiguity rule in `notes/chores-06.md > Generalize --scope ...`.\n    let template = match &args.use_template {\n        Some(s) => {\n            if s.contains(',') {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"--scope=app takes a single template path; got '{s}' (remove the `,BOT` half or use --scope=both)\"\n                )\n                .into());\n            }\n            let trimmed = s.trim();\n            if trimmed.is_empty() {\n                return Err(\"--use-template: code template path is empty\".into());\n            }\n            let code_t = PathBuf::from(trimmed);\n            if !code_t.exists() {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"--use-template: code template '{}' does not exist\",\n                    code_t.display()\n                )\n                .into());\n            }\n            if !code_t.is_dir() {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"--use-template: code template '{}' is not a directory\",\n                    code_t.display()\n                )\n                .into());\n            }\n            Some(code_t)\n        }\n        None => None,\n    };\n\n    let visibility = if args.private {\n        \"--private\"\n    } else {\n        \"--public\"\n    };\n\n    if args.dry_run {\n        info!(\"Dry run — would execute:\");\n        info!(\"  1. Create directory: {}\", project_dir.display());\n        info!(\"  2. git init + jj git init --colocate\");\n        info!(\"  3. Write .vc-config.toml (app-only) and .gitignore\");\n        match &template {\n            Some(c) => {\n                info!(\"  4. Copy template (non-hidden) + rewrite README.md first line\");\n                info!(\"       code: {}\", c.display());\n            }\n            None => info!(\"  4. (skipped — no --use-template)\"),\n        }\n        info!(\"  5. jj commit initial commit (no ochid trailer)\");\n        info!(\"  6. Set bookmark, remove jj, re-init jj\");\n        info!(\"  7. gh repo create {code_repo} {visibility}, push\");\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    // Step 1\n    info!(\"Step 1: Creating project directory...\");\n    mkdir_p(&project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 2\n    info!(\"Step 2: Initializing repo...\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 3\n    info!(\"Step 3: Writing config files...\");\n    write_file(&project_dir.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY)?;\n    write_file(&project_dir.join(\".gitignore\"), GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY)?;\n\n    // Step 4\n    if let Some(code_t) = &template {\n        info!(\"Step 4: Copying template...\");\n        info!(\"  code: {} -> {}\", code_t.display(), project_dir.display());\n        copy_template_recursive(code_t, &project_dir)?;\n        rewrite_readme_first_line(&project_dir, &args.name)?;\n    }\n\n    // Step 5: single repo, no ochid cross-reference needed.\n    info!(\"Step 5: Committing initial commit...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"commit\", \"-m\", \"Initial commit\"], &project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 6: set bookmark, strip jj, re-init jj so the git remote\n    // and jj bookmark tracking both land cleanly.\n    info!(\"Step 6: Setting bookmark and resetting jj...\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"git\", &[\"clean\", \"-xdf\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\"git\", &[\"checkout\", \"main\"], &project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 7: create GitHub repo and push.\n    let code_url = format!(\"git@github.com:{code_repo}.git\");\n    info!(\"Step 7: Creating GitHub repo {code_repo}...\");\n    run(\n        \"gh\",\n        &[\"repo\", \"create\", &code_repo, visibility],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n    run(\"git\", &[\"remote\", \"add\", \"origin\", &code_url], &project_dir)?;\n    run_retry(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"push\", \"-u\", \"origin\", \"main\"],\n        &project_dir,\n        args.push_retries,\n        args.push_retry_delay,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 8: re-initialize jj with remote tracking.\n    info!(\"Step 8: Re-initializing jj with remote tracking...\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"--quiet\", \"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &project_dir)?;\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"track\", \"main\", \"--remote=origin\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n    crate::common::verify_tracking(&project_dir, \"main\")?;\n    let code_chid_final = jj_chid(\"@-\", &project_dir)?;\n\n    info!(\"\");\n    info!(\"Done! Project created at {}\", project_dir.display());\n    info!(\"  Repo: {code_repo}  (chid={code_chid_final})\");\n\n    debug!(\"init_app: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {"
}

> DEVELOPER

hmm, actually we should do test-fixture first or we should allow `-R <path|url>` and support `file:///test-repo` or `/tmp/xxx` or `~/zzz` or `../def` or  `git@github.com:winksaville/test-repo`, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good catch — `init_app` as written can't be dogfooded without hitting real GitHub. Two ways out, and I lean toward the second:

**Option A — test-fixture first.** Build a `test-fixture --scope=app` (or similar) that stages a local bare repo as the "remote" and exercises `init_app` against it. Keeps `gh repo create` coupling in `init`; pushes the test-harness complexity.

**Option B — `-R <url|path>` on `init` (my pick).** Accept any git-remote-ish target as the origin: `/tmp/xxx.git`, `file:///tmp/xxx.git`, `~/zzz.git`, `../def.git`, `git@github.com:foo/bar.git`, `https://…`. When `-R` is given, skip `gh repo create` entirely and use the supplied URL as `origin`. When it's absent, keep today's behavior (`gh repo create` + derived SSH URL).

Why B:
1. Dogfood + local bare-repo testing fall out for free — no special test-fixture wiring.
2. Unblocks non-GitHub users (GitLab, self-hosted, offline) as a side effect.
3. GitHub coupling becomes a branch, not a requirement. Cleaner layering.
4. Orthogonal to `--scope` — works for `app` and `both` alike.

**Semantic note on the flag letter:** `-R`/`--repo` is already used project-wide for "which jj repo to operate on" (chid/desc/list/show/sync/fix-desc/validate-desc). Reusing `-R` on `init` for "where's the git remote" overloads the letter. I'd rename to `--remote <URL>` to keep the `-R` meaning consistent. Normalization: tilde expansion (`~/zzz` → `$HOME/zzz`), relative → absolute, `file://` stripped to a path. SSH URLs and `https://` pass through untouched. Ambiguity rule (`--remote` + auto-GitHub detection) → if `--remote` is set, we never call `gh repo create`, full stop.

**Impact on the in-flight `-1` step:** the `init_app` body I just wrote needs a `match --remote { Some → just set origin + push; None → current gh-create flow }` branch. ~30 extra lines, straightforward. Same structure applies (eventually) to `init_both` for symmetry, though I'd keep that out of `-1`.

**Revised plan for `-1`:**
- `--scope=app|other|both` on `init` (current work).
- `--remote <URL>` on `init` — active for `--scope=app` this step; for `--scope=both` the flag errors "not yet wired for --scope=both" (consistent with your "other commands it's an error" posture).
- Test: unit-level URL/path normalization; plus an end-to-end `init --scope=app --remote /tmp/...` run against a bare-repo fixture we set up inline.

If you're good with B, I'll extend the current working copy (already has `init_app` + `--scope`) with `--remote` support and finish step `-1` without committing yet. If you'd rather A, I'll roll back the `-1` edits unshipped and switch to test-fixture work.

> DEVELOPER

I like B too but your revised plan is backwards. I maybe we should add two options like --repo-local <path> and --repo-remote <url>, your thoughts no changes yet

> AGENT

You're right that my ordering is backwards — I was layering `--scope` on top of a still-GitHub-coupled `init`, then patching with `--remote` to make the new branch testable. The cleaner dependency order is: generalize the remote-ness first, then add scope on top of a flag set that already works.

**On the two flags:** like them. Clean separation of concerns:

- `--repo-local <path>` — where the on-disk repo goes. Today derived from positional `NAME` + `--dir`. With this flag you can skip that dance: `init --repo-local /tmp/myproj` and name is the basename.
- `--repo-remote <url>` — where `origin` points. Today derived from `owner/NAME` + `gh repo create`. With this flag, skip `gh` entirely and use the given URL directly (local bare repo, SSH, HTTPS, `file://`, `~/` — all normalized to absolute paths or passed through).

Defaults compose nicely: neither flag = today's behavior; either alone = that side explicit, other derived; both = fully qualified, positional `NAME` becomes optional (or an ambiguity error if also given).

**Dual-repo shape (`--scope=both`):** two locals, two remotes. Follow `--use-template`'s `CODE[,BOT]` pattern — `--repo-local CODE[,BOT]`, `--repo-remote CODE[,BOT]`, BOT derived via `.claude` suffix if omitted. Keeps the existing mental model.

**Flag name check:** `--repo-local` / `--repo-remote` reads well and doesn't collide with `-R`/`--repo` used elsewhere for jj-repo selection. The `repo-` prefix earns its keep.

**Ambiguity rules (pre-flight validation):**
- `NAME` + fully-qualified `--repo-local` → error (two ways to derive the basename).
- `--repo-remote` + `gh`-specific flags (`--owner`, `--private`) → error when `--repo-remote` skips GitHub.
- Dual-repo `--repo-remote CODE,BOT` where one side is a local path and the other is an SSH URL → allowed, just a mixed workspace. Don't over-restrict.

## Proposed step ordering

- **0.40.0-1 (revised)** — `--repo-local` + `--repo-remote` on `init`, dual-repo only. Current behavior preserved when both flags are absent. Bare-repo fixture tests land with this step. No `--scope` yet.

## Revised step `-1` plan

**`src/init.rs` surface changes:**

Two new flags on `InitArgs`:

- `--repo-local <PATH[,PATH]>` — on-disk placement. Single path in future `--scope=app`; `CODE[,BOT]` comma form today (dual-repo). BOT derives as `<CODE>.claude` when omitted (same rule as `--use-template`).
- `--repo-remote <URL[,URL]>` — `origin` URL for each repo. Same comma-form shape. When present, skip `gh repo create` entirely for that side.

**Make positional `NAME` optional.** Today it's required. With the new flags:
- `--repo-local` alone → derive NAME from basename of CODE.
- NAME alone → today's behavior (derive `<cwd>/<NAME>` and `<cwd>/<NAME>/.claude`).
- Both given → ambiguity error.
- Neither given → clap error ("NAME or --repo-local required").

**Normalization helper** `normalize_remote(s) -> String`:
- `~/...` expands via `$HOME`.
- Relative paths (`../x.git`, `./x.git`, `x.git`) → canonical absolute.
- `file:///abs` → pass through (git understands it natively).
- `http(s)://`, `ssh://`, `git@host:path` → pass through.
- Bare absolute paths (`/tmp/x.git`) → pass through.

**Preflight changes:**
- `gh auth status` only runs when at least one side needs `gh repo create` (i.e., `--repo-remote` absent for that side).
- `gh_whoami` called only when needed (same gate).
- `gh_repo_exists` precheck skipped per side when `--repo-remote` supplies that side.

**Ambiguity rules:**
- `NAME` + `--repo-local` → error.
- `--repo-remote` covers all sides + `--owner` → error (no GitHub derivation happening).
- `--repo-remote` covers all sides + `--private` → error (visibility is a `gh repo create` concept).
- `--repo-local A,B` with one path missing (`,B` or `A,`) → error.

**Refactor shape inside `init()` body:**
- Pre-resolve `(project_dir, session_dir)` from whichever source applies.
- Pre-resolve `(code_remote_url, session_remote_url)` — `Some(url)` if supplied, `None` means "we'll create via `gh`."
- Each `gh repo create` call + `gh_repo_exists` precheck becomes gated on the `None` case.
- `git remote add origin <url>` uses the supplied-or-derived URL uniformly.
- `debug!(…)` documentary lines above each `run()` call in the touched paths (new convention from your last instruction).

**Tests:**
- Clap parsing: `--repo-local=A`, `--repo-local=A,B`, `--repo-remote=URL`, `--repo-remote=URL1,URL2`, missing halves error.
- `normalize_remote` unit tests — each case above.
- Ambiguity rejections for NAME-plus-local, remote-plus-owner, remote-plus-private.
- **End-to-end:** create two bare `git init --bare` repos in tempdir, invoke `init` with `--repo-remote` pointing at them, assert: project tree exists, both `.vc-config.toml`s are correct, both commits are present in the bare repos, `origin` remotes match, jj bookmark tracking is set up. No `gh` invocation should happen — confirmed by running with `PATH` scrubbed of `gh` (or by asserting a mock not-called).

**`notes/chores-06.md`:**
- Amend the `## Generalize --scope across commands (0.40.0)` section's step list: `-1` becomes "init remote decoupling via `--repo-local` + `--repo-remote`"; `-2` becomes the scope work; later steps renumber.
- Append `### 0.40.0-1: init remote decoupling` subsection documenting what shipped.

**Out of scope for `-1`:** no `--scope` flag yet; no `Scope` enum; no `init_app`. All that lands in `-2`.

## Open questions

1. `NAME` optional: OK to make the positional optional this step? It's a small CLI-surface change but enables the clean "fully qualified via flags" case.
2. Tilde expansion: use `$HOME` or attempt a richer expansion (e.g. `~user/…`)? I'd stick to `$HOME` only — matches shell `~` with no username.
3. Should `--repo-local` + `--repo-remote` both taking `CODE[,BOT]` form be documented as "must match shape" (both give 1 value, or both give 2)? I think yes — mismatch is fatal per ambiguity rule.

If this looks right, say go and I'll implement.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/init.rs",
  "old_string": "use crate::common::{mkdir_p, run, write_file};\n\n/// Parse the `--use-template` value into `(code, bot)` template paths.",
  "new_string": "use crate::common::{mkdir_p, run, write_file};\n\n/// Expand `~` (home) and `$VAR` / `${VAR}` (env) substitutions in a\n/// user-supplied string. Bare `~` and `~/...` are expanded via `HOME`;\n/// unset env vars are a fatal error (silent empty substitution masks\n/// typos — aligned with init's overall strict-input posture). Any\n/// literal `$` not followed by a valid name stays as-is.\npub(crate) fn expand_vars(s: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let home_expanded = if s == \"~\" {\n        std::env::var(\"HOME\").map_err(|_| \"HOME is not set; cannot expand '~'\")?\n    } else if let Some(rest) = s.strip_prefix(\"~/\") {\n        let home = std::env::var(\"HOME\").map_err(|_| \"HOME is not set; cannot expand '~/'\")?;\n        format!(\"{home}/{rest}\")\n    } else {\n        s.to_string()\n    };\n\n    let mut out = String::with_capacity(home_expanded.len());\n    let mut chars = home_expanded.chars().peekable();\n    while let Some(c) = chars.next() {\n        if c != '$' {\n            out.push(c);\n            continue;\n        }\n        let (name, braced) = if chars.peek() == Some(&'{') {\n            chars.next(); // OK: peeked '{' above, next() must yield it\n            let mut name = String::new();\n            let mut closed = false;\n            for c2 in chars.by_ref() {\n                if c2 == '}' {\n                    closed = true;\n                    break;\n                }\n                name.push(c2);\n            }\n            if !closed {\n                return Err(format!(\"unterminated '${{…}}' in '{s}'\").into());\n            }\n            (name, true)\n        } else {\n            let mut name = String::new();\n            while let Some(&c2) = chars.peek() {\n                if c2.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c2 == '_' {\n                    name.push(c2);\n                    chars.next();\n                } else {\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n            (name, false)\n        };\n        if name.is_empty() {\n            // A bare `$` or `${}` — pass through literally.\n            out.push('$');\n            if braced {\n                out.push_str(\"{}\");\n            }\n            continue;\n        }\n        let val = std::env::var(&name)\n            .map_err(|_| format!(\"env var '${name}' is not set (in '{s}')\"))?;\n        out.push_str(&val);\n    }\n    Ok(out)\n}\n\n/// True when `s` looks like a git remote URL rather than a filesystem\n/// path. Covers the three forms git accepts as URLs: scheme-qualified\n/// (`scheme://...`) and SSH's scp-like form (`user@host:path`).\n/// Everything else is treated as a path (including `file://…` because\n/// that uses `://` too — its path portion isn't canonicalized).\npub(crate) fn is_remote_url(s: &str) -> bool {\n    if s.contains(\"://\") {\n        return true;\n    }\n    // scp-like: `user@host:path`. Require `@` before `:` and no `/`\n    // before the `@` (paths like `./a@b:c` shouldn't qualify).\n    if let Some(at) = s.find('@')\n        && let Some(colon) = s.find(':')\n        && at < colon\n        && !s[..at].contains('/')\n    {\n        return true;\n    }\n    false\n}\n\n/// Normalize a `--repo-local` value: expand variables, then make\n/// the result absolute (relative paths resolve against cwd). Paths\n/// do not need to exist yet — init creates them.\npub(crate) fn normalize_local_path(s: &str) -> Result<PathBuf, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let s = s.trim();\n    if s.is_empty() {\n        return Err(\"--repo-local: path is empty\".into());\n    }\n    let expanded = expand_vars(s)?;\n    let p = PathBuf::from(&expanded);\n    if p.is_absolute() {\n        Ok(p)\n    } else {\n        let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;\n        Ok(cwd.join(p))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Normalize a `--repo-remote` value: expand variables; for paths,\n/// canonicalize to absolute; for URLs (scheme-qualified or SSH\n/// scp-like), pass through after expansion.\npub(crate) fn normalize_remote(s: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let s = s.trim();\n    if s.is_empty() {\n        return Err(\"--repo-remote: URL is empty\".into());\n    }\n    let expanded = expand_vars(s)?;\n    if is_remote_url(&expanded) {\n        return Ok(expanded);\n    }\n    // Treat as filesystem path.\n    let p = PathBuf::from(&expanded);\n    if p.is_absolute() {\n        Ok(expanded)\n    } else {\n        let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;\n        Ok(cwd.join(p).to_string_lossy().to_string())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Split a `CODE,BOT` value into exactly two parts, both non-empty.\n/// Used by `--repo-local` and `--repo-remote` — neither derives the\n/// BOT half in dual-repo init (unlike `--use-template`), so each\n/// side must be explicit. A missing comma, trailing comma, leading\n/// comma, or more than one comma is a fatal error.\npub(crate) fn split_code_bot(\n    s: &str,\n    flag: &str,\n) -> Result<(String, String), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let parts: Vec<&str> = s.split(',').collect();\n    if parts.len() != 2 {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"{flag}: expected exactly two comma-separated values (CODE,BOT); got '{s}'\"\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    let code = parts[0].trim();\n    let bot = parts[1].trim();\n    if code.is_empty() || bot.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"{flag}: CODE and BOT must both be non-empty; got '{s}'\").into());\n    }\n    Ok((code.to_string(), bot.to_string()))\n}\n\n/// Parse the `--use-template` value into `(code, bot)` template paths."
}

> AGENT

I've been going in circles on the `CODE,BOT` shape question. Settling: `--repo-local` takes a **single** path (session nested at `PATH/.claude`, preserving `.vc-config.toml`'s hardcoded relative layout), and `--repo-remote` takes `CODE,BOT` (both URLs required, no derivation). The shapes are asymmetric because on-disk structure is nested (one root path suffices) while remote URLs are independent. Fixing the docstring and parse to match.

  "new_string": "/// Resolve the on-disk project root from either `--repo-local` or the\n/// positional `NAME` + optional `--dir`. Exactly one pathway must\n/// supply the placement — NAME and `--repo-local` are mutually\n/// exclusive (ambiguity rule), and `--dir` only composes with NAME\n/// (it would be redundant under `--repo-local`).\nfn resolve_project_dir(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<PathBuf, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match (&args.name, &args.repo_local) {\n        (Some(_), Some(_)) => Err(\n            \"NAME and --repo-local are mutually exclusive (both specify on-disk placement)\".into(),\n        ),\n        (None, None) => Err(\"NAME or --repo-local is required\".into()),\n        (Some(name), None) => {\n            let parent_dir = match &args.dir {\n                Some(d) => d.clone(),\n                None => std::env::current_dir()?,\n            };\n            Ok(parent_dir.join(name))\n        }\n        (None, Some(spec)) => {\n            if args.dir.is_some() {\n                return Err(\n                    \"--dir and --repo-local are mutually exclusive (both specify placement)\".into(),\n                );\n            }\n            if spec.contains(',') {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"--repo-local takes a single path; got '{spec}' (session side is always <PATH>/.claude)\"\n                )\n                .into());\n            }\n            normalize_local_path(spec)\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Resolve remote URLs from `--repo-remote` if present, normalizing\n/// both halves. Returns `None` when the flag is absent — the caller\n/// will derive URLs via `gh repo create`.\nfn resolve_remotes(\n    args: &InitArgs,\n) -> Result<Option<(String, String)>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match &args.repo_remote {\n        None => Ok(None),\n        Some(spec) => {\n            if args.owner.is_some() {\n                return Err(\"--repo-remote and --owner are mutually exclusive (no GitHub derivation happens when remotes are explicit)\".into());\n            }\n            if args.private {\n                return Err(\"--repo-remote and --private are mutually exclusive (--private only affects `gh repo create`)\".into());\n            }\n            let (code_raw, bot_raw) = split_code_bot(spec, \"--repo-remote\")?;\n            Ok(Some((\n                normalize_remote(&code_raw)?,\n                normalize_remote(&bot_raw)?,\n            )))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub fn init(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"init: enter\");\n\n    // Resolve placement + remote URLs from the flag combinations.\n    let project_dir = resolve_project_dir(args)?;\n    let session_dir = project_dir.join(\".claude\");\n    let explicit_remotes = resolve_remotes(args)?;\n\n    // Project name for README rewriting + (when needed) GitHub repo\n    // name derivation. Comes from basename of project_dir whether\n    // that was from NAME or --repo-local.\n    let name = project_dir\n        .file_name()\n        .ok_or_else(|| {\n            format!(\n                \"cannot derive project name from '{}'\",\n                project_dir.display()\n            )\n        })?\n        .to_string_lossy()\n        .to_string();\n    let session_name = format!(\"{name}.claude\");\n\n    // Preflight checks\n    info!(\"Preflight checks...\");\n\n    debug!(\"verify jj is installed\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"--version\"], Path::new(\".\")).map_err(|_| \"jj is not installed\")?;\n\n    let using_gh = explicit_remotes.is_none();\n    if using_gh {\n        debug!(\"verify gh CLI is installed + authenticated\");\n        run(\"gh\", &[\"auth\", \"status\"], Path::new(\".\"))\n            .map_err(|_| \"gh is not installed or not authenticated (run: gh auth login)\")?;\n    }\n\n    // GitHub owner is only meaningful when deriving URLs via gh.\n    let owner = if using_gh {\n        match &args.owner {\n            Some(o) => o.clone(),\n            None => gh_whoami()?,\n        }\n    } else {\n        String::new() // OK: unused when explicit_remotes is Some\n    };\n\n    if project_dir.exists() {\n        return Err(format!(\"'{}' already exists\", project_dir.display()).into());\n    }\n\n    // GitHub existence precheck only matters when we're about to\n    // create those repos. With --repo-remote, we assume the caller\n    // already has the target set up (or intends to receive the push).\n    let (code_repo, session_repo, code_url, session_url) = match &explicit_remotes {\n        None => {\n            let code_repo = format!(\"{owner}/{name}\");\n            let session_repo = format!(\"{owner}/{session_name}\");\n            if gh_repo_exists(&owner, &name)? {\n                return Err(format!(\"GitHub repo '{code_repo}' already exists\").into());\n            }\n            if gh_repo_exists(&owner, &session_name)? {\n                return Err(format!(\"GitHub repo '{session_repo}' already exists\").into());\n            }\n            let code_url = format!(\"git@github.com:{code_repo}.git\");\n            let session_url = format!(\"git@github.com:{session_repo}.git\");\n            (code_repo, session_repo, code_url, session_url)\n        }\n        Some((c, s)) => (\n            // Repo-name slots aren't used in log output when remotes\n            // are explicit — still populate for consistency.\n            String::new(),\n            String::new(),\n            c.clone(),\n            s.clone(),\n        ),\n    };\n\n    let templates = match &args.use_template {\n        Some(s) => {\n            let (code_t, bot_t) = parse_use_template(s)?;\n            validate_templates(&code_t, &bot_t)?;\n            Some((code_t, bot_t))\n        }\n        None => None,\n    };\n\n    let visibility = if args.private {\n        \"--private\"\n    } else {\n        \"--public\"\n    };\n\n    if args.dry_run {\n        info!(\"Dry run — would execute:\");\n        info!(\"  1. Create directories: {}\", project_dir.display());\n        info!(\"  2. git init + jj git init --colocate on both repos\");\n        info!(\"  3. Write .vc-config.toml and .gitignore to both\");\n        match &templates {\n            Some((c, b)) => {\n                info!(\n                    \"  4. Copy templates (non-hidden) into both repos + rewrite README.md first line\"\n                );\n                info!(\"       code: {}\", c.display());\n                info!(\"       bot:  {}\", b.display());\n            }\n            None => info!(\"  4. (skipped — no --use-template)\"),\n        }\n        info!(\"  5. jj commit both with placeholder ochids\");\n        info!(\"  6. Get both chids, jj describe both with correct ochids\");\n        info!(\"  7. Remove jj from both (git clean -xdf)\");\n        if using_gh {\n            info!(\"  8. gh repo create {session_repo} {visibility}, push to {session_url}\");\n            info!(\"  9. gh repo create {code_repo} {visibility}, push to {code_url}\");\n        } else {\n            info!(\"  8. skip gh repo create; push to {session_url}\");\n            info!(\"  9. skip gh repo create; push to {code_url}\");\n        }\n        info!(\"  10. jj git init --colocate on both repos\");\n        info!(\"  11. Create Claude Code symlink\");\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    // Step 1: Create directories\n    info!(\"Step 1: Creating project directories...\");\n    mkdir_p(&project_dir)?;\n    mkdir_p(&session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 2: git init + jj init on both repos\n    info!(\"Step 2: Initializing repos...\");\n    debug!(\"seed project_dir with an empty git repo\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], &project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"colocate jj atop the git repo so jj + git share .git state\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"seed session_dir with an empty git repo\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"init\"], &session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"colocate jj atop the session git repo\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"], &session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 3: Write config files\n    info!(\"Step 3: Writing config files...\");\n    write_file(&project_dir.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), VC_CONFIG_CODE)?;\n    write_file(&project_dir.join(\".gitignore\"), GITIGNORE_CODE)?;\n    write_file(&session_dir.join(\".vc-config.toml\"), VC_CONFIG_SESSION)?;\n    write_file(&session_dir.join(\".gitignore\"), GITIGNORE_SESSION)?;\n\n    // Step 4: Copy templates (if --use-template)\n    if let Some((code_t, bot_t)) = &templates {\n        info!(\"Step 4: Copying templates...\");\n        info!(\"  code: {} -> {}\", code_t.display(), project_dir.display());\n        copy_template_recursive(code_t, &project_dir)?;\n        rewrite_readme_first_line(&project_dir, &name)?;\n        info!(\"  bot:  {} -> {}\", bot_t.display(), session_dir.display());\n        copy_template_recursive(bot_t, &session_dir)?;\n        rewrite_readme_first_line(&session_dir, &session_name)?;\n    }\n\n    // Step 5: jj commit both with placeholder ochids\n    info!(\"Step 5: Committing both repos with placeholder ochids...\");\n    debug!(\"code side: first commit with placeholder ochid (rewritten in step 6 once session chid known)\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"commit\", \"-m\", \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"session side: first commit with placeholder ochid (rewritten in step 6)\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"commit\", \"-m\", \"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /none\"],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 6: Get both chids, then describe both with correct ochids\n    info!(\"Step 6: Setting ochid cross-references...\");\n    let code_chid = jj_chid(\"@-\", &project_dir)?;\n    let session_chid = jj_chid(\"@-\", &session_dir)?;\n\n    let code_desc = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /.claude/{session_chid}\");\n    let session_desc = format!(\"Initial commit\\n\\nochid: /{code_chid}\");\n    debug!(\"code side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at session chid\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"describe\", \"@-\", \"-m\", &code_desc], &project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"session side: rewrite initial commit's ochid to point at code chid\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"describe\", \"@-\", \"-m\", &session_desc], &session_dir)?;\n\n    {\n        debug!(\"sanity: surface both post-describe git hashes for the debug log\");\n        let hash = run(\"git\", &[\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"], &project_dir)?;\n        debug!(\"code repo: chid={code_chid} hash={hash}\");\n        let hash = run(\"git\", &[\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"], &session_dir)?;\n        debug!(\".claude:   chid={session_chid} hash={hash}\");\n    }\n\n    // Step 7: Set bookmarks (creates git branches), then remove jj\n    // Bookmarks must be set before removing .jj/ so git has a 'main' branch to push\n    info!(\"Step 7: Setting bookmarks and removing jj...\");\n    debug!(\"place code-side main bookmark at the initial commit\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"place session-side main bookmark at the initial commit\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"strip jj state from session side before its git push\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"clean\", \"-xdf\"], &session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"strip jj state from code side, preserving nested .claude/\");\n    run(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"clean\", \"-xdf\", \"--exclude\", \".claude\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"re-attach session HEAD to main after clean\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"checkout\", \"main\"], &session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"re-attach code HEAD to main after clean\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"checkout\", \"main\"], &project_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 8: Session-side remote + push.\n    if using_gh {\n        info!(\"Step 8: Creating GitHub repo {session_repo}...\");\n        debug!(\"create session-side remote on GitHub\");\n        run(\n            \"gh\",\n            &[\"repo\", \"create\", &session_repo, visibility],\n            &project_dir,\n        )?;\n    } else {\n        info!(\"Step 8: Using supplied session remote {session_url}\");\n    }\n    debug!(\"point session-side git at its remote\");\n    run(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"remote\", \"add\", \"origin\", &session_url],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"publish session-side initial commit; retry since a fresh gh repo may need a moment\");\n    run_retry(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"push\", \"-u\", \"origin\", \"main\"],\n        &session_dir,\n        args.push_retries,\n        args.push_retry_delay,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 9: Code-side remote + push.\n    if using_gh {\n        info!(\"Step 9: Creating GitHub repo {code_repo}...\");\n        debug!(\"create code-side remote on GitHub\");\n        run(\n            \"gh\",\n            &[\"repo\", \"create\", &code_repo, visibility],\n            &project_dir,\n        )?;\n    } else {\n        info!(\"Step 9: Using supplied code remote {code_url}\");\n    }\n    debug!(\"point code-side git at its remote\");\n    run(\"git\", &[\"remote\", \"add\", \"origin\", &code_url], &project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"publish code-side initial commit; retry since a fresh gh repo may need a moment\");\n    run_retry(\n        \"git\",\n        &[\"push\", \"-u\", \"origin\", \"main\"],\n        &project_dir,\n        args.push_retries,\n        args.push_retry_delay,\n    )?;\n\n    // Step 10: Re-initialize jj on both repos\n    info!(\"Step 10: Re-initializing jj on both repos...\");\n    debug!(\"re-colocate jj atop the now-remote-linked code repo\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"--quiet\", \"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"restore code-side main bookmark after re-init\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &project_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"enable jj tracking of code-side main against origin\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"track\", \"main\", \"--remote=origin\"],\n        &project_dir,\n    )?;\n    crate::common::verify_tracking(&project_dir, \"main\")?;\n    let code_chid_final = jj_chid(\"@-\", &project_dir)?;\n\n    debug!(\"re-colocate jj atop the now-remote-linked session repo\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"--quiet\", \"git\", \"init\", \"--colocate\"],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n    debug!(\"restore session-side main bookmark after re-init\");\n    run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@-\"], &session_dir)?;\n    debug!(\"enable jj tracking of session-side main against origin\");\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"bookmark\", \"track\", \"main\", \"--remote=origin\"],\n        &session_dir,\n    )?;\n    crate::common::verify_tracking(&session_dir, \"main\")?;\n    let session_chid_final = jj_chid(\"@-\", &session_dir)?;\n\n    // Step 11: Create Claude Code symlink\n    info!(\"Step 11: Creating Claude Code symlink...\");\n    let symlink_dir = {\n        let home =\n            std::env::var(\"HOME\").map_err(|_| \"HOME environment variable not set\".to_string())?;\n        PathBuf::from(home).join(\".claude\").join(\"projects\")\n    };\n\n    let sl = symlink::SymLink::new(&project_dir, Path::new(\".claude\"), &symlink_dir)?;\n    sl.create(false)?;\n\n    info!(\"\");\n    info!(\"Done! Project created at {}\", project_dir.display());\n    info!(\"  Code repo:    {code_url}  (chid={code_chid_final})\");\n    info!(\"  Session repo: {session_url}  (chid={session_chid_final})\");\n    info!(\n        \"  Symlink:      {} -> {}\",\n        sl.symlink_path.display(),\n        sl.abs_target.display()\n    );\n\n    debug!(\"init: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}"

assert!(split_code_bot(\"a,\", \"--flag\").is_err());\n        assert!(split_code_bot(\",b\", \"--flag\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn expand_vars_tilde() {\n        // SAFETY: tests in this crate run single-threaded by default\n        // when invoked via `cargo test`, but env-var manipulation is\n        // genuinely racy with parallel runners. Set HOME to a known\n        // value, capture via the helper, then restore.\n        let prev = std::env::var(\"HOME\").ok();\n        // SAFETY: see comment above.\n        unsafe {\n            std::env::set_var(\"HOME\", \"/h\");\n        }\n        assert_eq!(expand_vars(\"~/foo\").unwrap(), \"/h/foo\");\n        assert_eq!(expand_vars(\"~\").unwrap(), \"/h\");\n        // Restore.\n        // SAFETY: see comment above.\n        unsafe {\n            match prev {\n                Some(v) => std::env::set_var(\"HOME\", v),\n                None => std::env::remove_var(\"HOME\"),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn expand_vars_envvar() {\n        let key = \"VC_X1_TEST_EXPAND_VAR\";\n        // SAFETY: see expand_vars_tilde test.\n        unsafe {\n            std::env::set_var(key, \"VAL\");\n        }\n        assert_eq!(expand_vars(\"$VC_X1_TEST_EXPAND_VAR/x\").unwrap(), \"VAL/x\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            expand_vars(\"a${VC_X1_TEST_EXPAND_VAR}b\").unwrap(),\n            \"aVALb\"\n        );\n        // Bare $ stays literal.\n        assert_eq!(expand_vars(\"$/no-name\").unwrap(), \"$/no-name\");\n        // Unset env var errors loudly.\n        assert!(expand_vars(\"$VC_X1_TEST_DEFINITELY_UNSET_xyz\").is_err());\n        // SAFETY: see expand_vars_tilde test.\n        unsafe {\n            std::env::remove_var(key);\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn expand_vars_unterminated_brace_errors() {\n        assert!(expand_vars(\"${UNCLOSED\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn is_remote_url_classifies() {\n        assert!(is_remote_url(\"https://host/path\"));\n        assert!(is_remote_url(\"ssh://git@host/path\"));\n        assert!(is_remote_url(\"file:///abs/path\"));\n        assert!(is_remote_url(\"git@github.com:owner/repo.git\"));\n        assert!(!is_remote_url(\"/tmp/x.git\"));\n        assert!(!is_remote_url(\"./x.git\"));\n        assert!(!is_remote_url(\"../x.git\"));\n        assert!(!is_remote_url(\"plain/relative/path\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_remote_url_passthrough() {\n        let s = \"git@github.com:user/foo.git\";\n        assert_eq!(normalize_remote(s).unwrap(), s);\n        let s = \"https://example.com/x.git\";\n        assert_eq!(normalize_remote(s).unwrap(), s);\n        let s = \"file:///tmp/x.git\";\n        assert_eq!(normalize_remote(s).unwrap(), s);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_remote_path_absolute_passthrough() {\n        let s = \"/tmp/foo.git\";\n        assert_eq!(normalize_remote(s).unwrap(), s);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_remote_path_relative_canonicalized() {\n        let cwd = std::env::current_dir().unwrap();\n        let got = normalize_remote(\"./foo.git\").unwrap();\n        assert!(got.starts_with(&cwd.to_string_lossy().to_string()));\n        assert!(got.ends_with(\"foo.git\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_remote_empty_errors() {\n        assert!(normalize_remote(\"\").is_err());\n        assert!(normalize_remote(\"   \").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_local_path_absolute() {\n        let p = normalize_local_path(\"/tmp/foo\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(p, PathBuf::from(\"/tmp/foo\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_local_path_relative_canonicalized() {\n        let cwd = std::env::current_dir().unwrap();\n        let p = normalize_local_path(\"./bar\").unwrap();\n        assert!(p.starts_with(&cwd));\n        assert!(p.ends_with(\"bar\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn normalize_local_path_empty_errors() {\n        assert!(normalize_local_path(\"\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    // ---------- Ambiguity rejections in init() dispatch ----------\n\n    /// Build an `InitArgs` with the given overrides; everything else\n    /// stays at safe defaults so the dispatcher's pre-flight rules\n    /// are exercised in isolation.\n    fn fixture(\n        name: Option<&str>,\n        repo_local: Option<&str>,\n        dir: Option<&str>,\n        repo_remote: Option<&str>,\n        owner: Option<&str>,\n        private: bool,\n    ) -> InitArgs {\n        InitArgs {\n            name: name.map(str::to_string),\n            owner: owner.map(str::to_string),\n            dir: dir.map(PathBuf::from),\n            private,\n            dry_run: true,\n            push_retries: 5,\n            push_retry_delay: 3,\n            use_template: None,\n            repo_local: repo_local.map(str::to_string),\n            repo_remote: repo_remote.map(str::to_string),\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ambig_name_and_repo_local() {\n        let args = fixture(Some(\"x\"), Some(\"/tmp/x\"), None, None, None, false);\n        let err = init(&args).unwrap_err().to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"NAME\"));\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--repo-local\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ambig_neither_name_nor_repo_local() {\n        let args = fixture(None, None, None, None, None, false);\n        let err = init(&args).unwrap_err().to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--repo-local\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ambig_dir_and_repo_local() {\n        let args = fixture(None, Some(\"/tmp/x\"), Some(\"/tmp/parent\"), None, None, false);\n        let err = init(&args).unwrap_err().to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--dir\"));\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--repo-local\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ambig_repo_remote_and_owner() {\n        let args = fixture(\n            Some(\"x\"),\n            None,\n            None,\n            Some(\"/tmp/c.git,/tmp/s.git\"),\n            Some(\"user\"),\n            false,\n        );\n        let err = init(&args).unwrap_err().to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--repo-remote\"));\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--owner\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ambig_repo_remote_and_private() {\n        let args = fixture(\n            Some(\"x\"),\n            None,\n            None,\n            Some(\"/tmp/c.git,/tmp/s.git\"),\n            None,\n            true,\n        );\n        let err = init(&args).unwrap_err().to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--repo-remote\"));\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--private\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn repo_local_with_comma_errors() {\n        let args = fixture(None, Some(\"/tmp/a,/tmp/b\"), None, None, None, false);\n        let err = init(&args).unwrap_err().to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"--repo-local\"));\n    }\n}"

807	## Generalize --scope across commands (0.40.0)
808	
809	Foundation for item #1 on todo ([60]): commands should support
810	single-repo, dual-repo, and plain-old-repo (POR) workspaces
811	without hard-coding the `.claude` dual-repo assumption.
812	
813	**Scope default resolution** (no `--scope` given):
814	
815	- No `.vc-config.toml` → `--scope=app`.
816	- `.vc-config.toml` present but `other-repo` missing or empty
817	  → `--scope=app`.
818	- `.vc-config.toml` with a non-empty `other-repo` → `--scope=both`.
819	
820	**Ambiguity rule.** Any ambiguous combination of scope-related
821	flags on a single invocation (e.g. `-R` with `--scope`) is a
822	fatal error — callers must pick one way to express repo
823	selection per call.
824	
825	**Cycle steps.**
826	
827	- **0.40.0-0** — this plan. Notes + version bump only.
828	- **0.40.0-1** — `vc-x1 init --scope=app|other|both`.
829	  - `app` → single-repo workspace (`.vc-config.toml` with
830	    `path = "/"` and no `other-repo`; no `.claude` subdir; one
831	    GitHub repo; no `ochid:` trailer on the initial commit).
832	  - `both` (default) → current dual-repo behavior.
833	  - `other` → error; meaningless at init time.
834	  - Every other subcommand errors loudly if `--scope` is passed.
835	- Subsequent `-N` steps wire `--scope` into sync, push,
836	  finalize, and the read-only commands. Specifics decided when
837	  each lands.
838	
839	**Helper placement is deliberately open** — `common.rs`,
840	`init.rs`, or a dedicated `scope.rs` / `scope_helpers.rs` are
841	all fine for step `-1`; we can refactor later once more call
842	sites exist. The plan is subject to change as we learn; `-0`
843	records the current best guess, not a contract.
844	
845	# References
846	
847	[57]: /notes/chores-05.md#capture-squash-mode--scope-design-for-push-0374
848	[60]: #generalize---scope-to-all-commands-design
849	[61]: #push-hardening-state--stage-sanity-design
850	[63]: #non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design
851	[64]: #notes-restructure-chores-06--trim-long-todo-entries-0377
852	[65]: #scope-design-refinements-0378
853	[66]: #bookmark-tracking-verification-0380
854	[67]: #push-hardening-state--stage-sanity-0390
855	[68]: #source-code-design-ref-convention-design
856	[69]: #vc-x1-validate-repo-command-design

Specifics decided when\n  each lands.\n\n**Helper placement is deliberately open** — `common.rs`,\n`init.rs`, or a dedicated `scope.rs` / `scope_helpers.rs` are\nall fine for step `-1`; we can refactor later once more call\nsites exist.   "new_string": "**Cycle steps.**\n\n- **0.40.0-0** — this plan. Notes + version bump only.\n- **0.40.0-1** — `vc-x1 init --repo-local` + `--repo-remote`.\n  Decouple init from `gh repo create` so it can target any git\n  remote (local bare repo, `file:///…`, SSH, HTTPS) — both as a\n  step toward POR / single-repo support and so dogfooding +\n  testing can run without hitting real GitHub. Dual-repo only;\n  no `--scope` yet. **Ordering rationale (added 2026-04-24):**\n  doing `--scope=app` before this step would build on a still\n  GitHub-coupled init and immediately need rework once\n  `--repo-remote` lands; flipping the order means `-2` lands\n  cleanly on top of an already remote-agnostic flow.\n- **0.40.0-2** — `vc-x1 init --scope=app|other|both`.\n  - `app` → single-repo workspace (`.vc-config.toml` with\n    `path = \"/\"` and no `other-repo`; no `.claude` subdir; one\n    GitHub repo (or one `--repo-remote`); no `ochid:` trailer\n    on the initial commit).\n  - `both` (default) → current dual-repo behavior.\n  - `other` → error; meaningless at init time.\n  - Every other subcommand errors loudly if `--scope` is passed.\n- Subsequent `-N` steps wire `--scope` into sync, push,\n  finalize, and the read-only commands. Specifics decided when\n  each lands.\n\n**Helper placement is deliberately open** — `common.rs`,\n`init.rs`, or a dedicated `scope.rs` / `scope_helpers.rs` are\nall fine; we can refactor later once more call sites exist. The\nplan is subject to change as we learn; `-0` records the current\nbest guess, not a contract.\n\n### 0.40.0-1: init remote decoupling\n\nTwo new flags on `vc-x1 init` to break the hard coupling to\n`gh repo create`:\n\n- `--repo-local <PATH>` — single path; replaces the on-disk\n  project root. Session repo always lands at `<PATH>/.claude`\n  (the nested layout is hardcoded in `.vc-config.toml`'s\n  `other-repo = \".claude\"` and `path = \"/.claude\"` fields).\n  Mutually exclusive with the positional `NAME` and `--dir`.\n  The positional `NAME` is now optional at the clap layer; the\n  dispatcher errors at runtime if neither is supplied.\n- `--repo-remote <CODE,BOT>` — both URLs explicit (no\n  derivation; URL conventions vary too much to magic-derive).\n  Each value may be a bare path, `file:///…`, an HTTPS URL, or\n  an SSH URL (`git@host:path`). `~/…` and `$VAR` / `${VAR}`\n  are expanded; relative paths canonicalize against cwd; URLs\n  pass through. Mutually exclusive with `--owner` and\n  `--private` (both are `gh repo create`-only concepts). When\n  `--repo-remote` is present, init no longer requires the `gh`\n  CLI to be installed or authenticated.\n\n**Asymmetric value shapes are intentional.** `--repo-local`\ntakes one path because the on-disk layout is nested.\n`--repo-remote` takes two because remote URLs are independent.\nThere is no shape-matching rule between the two flags.\n\n**Helper module is `init.rs`.** No `scope.rs` yet — that lands\nin `-2`. Helpers added: `expand_vars`, `is_remote_url`,\n`normalize_local_path`, `normalize_remote`, `split_code_bot`,\nplus two private resolvers `resolve_project_dir` and\n`resolve_remotes`.\n\n**Documentary `debug!` lines** — every `run()` invocation in\nthe touched paths now has a `debug!(…)` immediately above it\nthat captures the *intent* of the call (the *action* is logged\nby `common::run` itself). Exists in source as documentation\nand in the `-v` log as runtime breadcrumbs. New convention\n(2026-04-24); applied to the `init()` body in this step,\nretrofit-able elsewhere later.\n\n- `Cargo.toml`: bump to `0.40.0-1`.\n- `src/init.rs`: new flags, helpers, dispatcher refactor, and\n  documentary `debug!` retrofit.\n- `notes/todo.md`: ref bookkeeping unchanged (item still in\n  progress under `[60],[70]`).\n- `notes/chores-06.md`: amend `-0` plan ordering rationale +\n  this subsection."
}

> AGENT

Got it — I had `--repo-local` completely wrong. Restating the corrected semantics to make sure we're aligned:

## Corrected design

**`--repo-local <PARENT>`** — fully self-contained workspace, no GitHub, no external `gh` calls. Creates:
- `<PARENT>/<NAME>/` — project (code side)
- `<PARENT>/<NAME>/.claude/` — session (nested as today)
- `<PARENT>/remote-code.git` — bare repo, used as code's `origin`
- `<PARENT>/remote-claude.git` — bare repo, used as session's `origin`

**`--repo-remote <URL>`** — single URL stem; both sides derived. `<URL>.git` for code, `<URL>.claude.git` for session. Skips `gh repo create` entirely; the URL must already point at writable repos. So `vc-x1 init tf1 --repo-remote git@github.com:winksaville/tf1` is exactly today's `vc-x1 init tf1` (when owner=`winksaville`) — just lets you spell the URL when the default derivation doesn't fit.

**Default (neither flag)** — today's behavior: `gh whoami` + `gh repo create` + derived SSH URL.

**Mutually exclusive.** `--repo-local` and `--repo-remote` together → fatal. NAME stays required in all three modes.

## On retiring `test-fixture`

Yes — once `--repo-local` lands, `test-fixture` is largely redundant. But I'd not bundle the removal into `-1`:

- `test-fixture` has callers throughout `src/sync.rs` and `src/push.rs` integration tests. Migrating those to `init --repo-local` is its own change with its own risk surface.
- There may be subtle differences: `test-fixture` creates a fixture with specific bookmark state and bare-remote layout that the integration tests assume. `init --repo-local` makes one initial commit. We'll need to verify the structures match before swapping.
- Step `-1` should ship one decoupling change cleanly — adding the flags. Migrating callers is a follow-up.

**Suggested cycle re-shape:**
- **0.40.0-1** (revised once more): `--repo-local PARENT` + `--repo-remote URL` on `init`, with the corrected semantics above. Mutually exclusive. NAME required.
- **0.40.0-2**: `--scope=app|other|both` (was the original `-1`).
- **0.40.0-3**: migrate `sync` and `push` integration tests from `test_fixture` to `init --repo-local`. Verify equivalence per test; adjust where shapes differ.
- **0.40.0-4**: delete the `test_fixture` module (and the `test-fixture` / `test-fixture-rm` subcommands) once no callers remain.
- Subsequent steps: wire `--scope` into sync, push, finalize, etc. (as before).

Test-fixture retirement is two steps because (a) migrating tests is the risky bit and (b) keeping module + commands alive across one step lets us run both old and new harnesses side-by-side if anything looks off during the migration.

## Path forward for the current working copy

The flags and dispatch logic I just wrote have the wrong shapes — value parsing, the `split_code_bot` helper, the resolution functions, and most of the new tests are all built around `CODE,BOT` two-value flags. Cheaper to roll back to `0.40.0-0` state and redo than to mutate in place. The helpers `expand_vars`, `is_remote_url`, and `normalize_remote` survive (still useful); `normalize_local_path` and `split_code_bot` come out.

> DEVELOPER

Do you agree for me, winksaville, `vc-x1 init --repo-remote git@github.com:winksaville/tf1` == `vc-x1 init tf1 --owner winksaville` == `vc-x1 init tf1`?

> AGENT

Almost — but there's a subtle wedge depending on how `--repo-remote` handles `gh repo create`.

**End-state equivalence (after init):** Yes — same project tree on disk, same remote URLs, same pushed commits.

**Step semantics:** Differ depending on a design call:

- `vc-x1 init tf1` → derives `git@github.com:winksaville/tf1.git`; calls `gh repo create`; pushes.
- `vc-x1 init tf1 --owner winksaville` → same as above (just skips `gh whoami`).
- `vc-x1 init --repo-remote git@github.com:winksaville/tf1` → uses the URL directly; **whether it calls `gh repo create` is a design call**.

Two ways to resolve the wedge:

**(a) Make them strictly equivalent.** Treat default as syntactic sugar over `--repo-remote <derived-URL>`. `--repo-remote` then has to detect GitHub URLs by host and call `gh repo create` for them (skipping for non-GitHub URLs and for paths). NAME is derived from the URL's last path segment (strip trailing `.git`) when not given positionally. This is what makes #1 == #2 == #3 hold literally.

**(b) Keep `--repo-remote` as a "bring your own remote" escape hatch.** Always skip `gh repo create` when `--repo-remote` is set; the URL must already point at a writable repo. Then #1 != #2/#3 — same end-state IFF the user pre-created the remote, but the steps `init` runs differ.

I lean toward **(a)**, because:
1. It collapses default + `--repo-remote` + `--owner` into one code path with parameters, removing the "two modes" mental model.
2. URL host detection is small (`s.starts_with("git@github.com:") || s.contains("://github.com/")`).
3. Non-GitHub URL handling falls out naturally — no magic for those, user pre-creates.
4. NAME derivation from URL ("derive when omitted, error if both given and they disagree") is a clean ambiguity rule.

The cost is one extra branch (URL-is-github) in the `should-I-call-gh-repo-create` check. Worth it for the unified semantics.

Under (a), the answer to your question is **yes, all three are equivalent** for winksaville.

Under (b), the answer is **same final remote URLs, but `gh repo create` runs in #2/#3 only**.

Which way do you want it?

> DEVELOPER

I'm think (a) too, but also I'd like this to work on gitlab or any other git based system local or remote. In the short term you might have to "pre-create" the repo itself but that would be ok too, thoughts?

> AGENT

Yes — option (a) with a tiered provisioner pattern. Making the design explicitly extensible:

## Unified model

The URL (whether given or derived) runs through a **provisioner dispatch**:

```
provisioner(url, repo_local) =
    if repo_local          → LocalBareInit    (git init --bare <parent>/remote-*.git)
    if is_github_url(url)  → GhCreate         (gh repo create)
    else                   → ExternalPreExisting  (skip — user pre-created)
```

**Default case** becomes syntactic sugar: `init tf1` ≡ `init tf1 --repo-remote git@github.com:<gh-whoami>/tf1`. Same code path, same dispatch.

**Host detection** for step `-1` is literal: GitHub (SSH + HTTPS). Future hosts (gitlab, gitea, self-hosted) slot in as additional arms — no restructuring needed. Short-term, non-GitHub users pre-create; that's fine. A `glab repo create` arm can land whenever someone needs it.

**Preflight per provisioner:**
- `GhCreate` — `gh auth status`, then `gh_repo_exists` (error if repo already exists, same as today).
- `LocalBareInit` — parent dir must exist; the bare-repo paths must NOT exist (refuse to clobber).
- `ExternalPreExisting` — for path-shaped URLs, verify the path exists (error with "use `--repo-local` for fixture creation, or pre-create with `git init --bare`"). For scheme-qualified URLs, no preflight — git push will surface failure.

## Answer to your equivalence question

Under this model, **yes** — #1 == #2 == #3 for winksaville. Same resolved URLs, same dispatch (both derive a GitHub URL, both hit `GhCreate`), same end state.

## Step `-1` implementation shape

**`--repo-local <PARENT>`** — fixture mode. Creates `<PARENT>/<NAME>/`, `<PARENT>/<NAME>/.claude/`, `<PARENT>/remote-code.git` (bare), `<PARENT>/remote-claude.git` (bare). Uses those bare repos as origins. No gh, no network.

**`--repo-remote <URL>`** — single URL stem. Session URL derived per rule: if URL ends in `.git`, insert `.claude` before the suffix; otherwise append `.claude`. NAME derived from URL's last path segment when positional omitted; if both given and they disagree, fatal.

**Mutually exclusive.** `--repo-local` + `--repo-remote` → error.

**NAME derivation rule:**
- URL last segment (after last `/` or `:`), stripped of trailing `.git`.
- `git@github.com:winksaville/tf1` → `tf1`
- `git@github.com:winksaville/tf1.git` → `tf1`
- `https://github.com/winksaville/tf1.git` → `tf1`
- `/tmp/foo.git` → `foo`

**Helpers (keep from last round):** `expand_vars`, `is_remote_url`, `normalize_remote`.
**Helpers (new):** `is_github_url`, `derive_session_url`, `derive_name_from_url`, `Provisioner` enum + dispatch.
**Helpers (drop from last round):** `normalize_local_path` (replaced by fixture-parent handling), `split_code_bot` (no pair form anymore).

## Cycle ordering (unchanged from last proposal)

- **0.40.0-1** (revised again, for real this time): `--repo-local` + `--repo-remote` with unified provisioner.
- **0.40.0-2**: `--scope=app|other|both`.
- **0.40.0-3**: migrate sync/push integration tests from `test_fixture` to `init --repo-local`.
- **0.40.0-4**: delete `test_fixture` module + subcommands.

If this all reads right, I'll roll back to `0.40.0-0` state and redo `-1` with the corrected semantics. Want me to?
